# CDP6 — Concept: a single- and multi-agent lifecycle framework for Claude Code

**Version:** 0.4.7
**As of:** October 2026 (2026-10-28, final revision after the closure checks cc3 on v0.4.6 and the owner decision on Q-15, Q-16 and closing the concept, §0 item 20; responses in §14.8; no owner question open in §15)
**Status:** proposed; counts as accepted once the orchestrator has verified v0.4.7 (§0 item 20 (3)). Nothing in this document is decided except the owner decisions quoted in §0; every choice is listed under "Decisions" with its alternatives and the null option. Decider for every choice: the owner (human).
**Scope:** CDP6 is a framework of skills, agents, hooks and deterministic checkers for Claude Code that carries one `intake` (an idea, a feature request, an issue or a bug report, all treated alike) through the full engineering cycle (idea, exploration, concept, refinement, implementation, tests and verification, retro, loop back to exploration) in three modes of equal standing: one session (single-agent mode), an orchestrating main session with spoke subagents (multi-agent mode), and a team lead with conversing teammates (team mode); each mode enforces the same contract, counters, captures and phase gates (ledger 0021 acceptance G1b), and the cycle can run unattended up to a human-set autonomy level. It lives in its own plugin area (`plugins/cdp6-core`) with its own agent and skill namespace, hooks, data directory and doctrine document. **Not included:** changes to the CDP5 plugins (`cdp5-agents`, `cdp5-gates`, `domain-agents`, `orbit-workflow` stay as they are); domain packs (psy, marketing, UX); hosts other than Claude Code; publication steps (push, merge, release, external messages), which stay with the human at every autonomy level; the code itself (this step names the files to be built and builds none).

## 0. Assignment (owner, verbatim, 2026-10-27)

1. "ok dann geh die Probleme mit den Counter-Roles und Experten an und geht alle offenen Punkte an, findet eine einheitliche und von jedem akzeptierte Lösung und setze diese um. Überführe die Punkte wo ST uns voraus ist ebenfalls in unser System und passe es auf unsere bedürfnisse an."
2. "/goal CDP Rework Version 6 -> Ein Multi- und Singleagent Framework für die Arbeit mit KI. Das Framework deckt den Ingeneursweg voll ab (Idee -> Exploration -> Konzept -> Refinement -> Umsetzung -> Tests -> Retro und dann wieder zurück zur Exploration) und kann je nach Anforderungen den kompletten Entwicklungszyklus autonom übernehmen. Nimm dir Supreme Team und andere Agentic Frameworks als Beispiel und behandel sie als eine Idee die Finalisiert werden soll. Du hast es erfüllt, wenn alle Arbeiten ohne drift und im Rahmen ihrer Anweisungen abgelaufen, anweisungen nicht übersehen oder missachtet wurden und jede Phase in sich geschlossen Funktioniert und alle Aufgaben und Anforderungen daraus erfüllt sind."

## 1. Purpose, plugin area and relation to CDP5

### 1.1 What CDP6 adds over CDP5

CDP5 covers concept, implementation, verification and retro with named agents, T1 skills and durable paths, but the doctrine says of itself "CDP5 is **not a phase workflow**" (doctrine §0.2, line 39). The idea phase has no role, skill or gate; exploration and loop-back are fragments; of 108 normative rules 12 are machine-checked, 22 partly and 74 are judgment only (E2 inventory). CDP6 turns the cycle into phases with entry and exit conditions on disk, a guard that enforces their order, a gate per phase with a deterministic part and a judgment part, and an enforcement status per rule. Its target: every rule whose failure costs data, a security boundary or silent drift is machine-checked by a named checker; the rest names its counter-role.

### 1.2 The plugin

One plugin, `plugins/cdp6-core` (choice CH-1). Agent namespace `cdp6-core:<role>`, skill namespace `cdp6-core:<skill>`, own `hooks/hooks.json`, own data directory (`CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` of `cdp6-core`; the pointer file `~/.cache/cdp6-core/data-dir` serves read-only scripts such as `cdp6-doctor`, while `run.mjs` takes the directory only from the `--data-dir` argument that `guard-shell.mjs` appends, UC-09 step 1, r5-DA O5-2), own doctrine `plugins/cdp6-core/reference/cdp6-doctrine.md`. Marketplace entry in `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` next to the four CDP5-era plugins; CDP6 declares `cdp5-gates` as a required sibling for the referenced T1 checkers (CH-2).

Layout (files to be built; `<P>` = `plugins/cdp6-core`):

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| `<P>/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` | name `cdp6-core`, version, description |
| `<P>/hooks/hooks.json` | hook registrations, matchers limited to the `cdp6-core:` namespace or to an active CDP6 run (§1.4); `guard-phase.mjs` also registered on PreToolUse, PermissionRequest and PermissionDenied with the catch-all matcher `"*"` (hooks.md:279), so every tool call of a run gets a guard line (UC-10 step 6; built in SL-8, CH-46); `guard-shell.mjs` registered on `Bash\|PowerShell\|Monitor`, every tool that runs a shell command under the sandbox (§1.4 item 10); every guard entry carries `"onFailure": "block"` (§5.4) |
| `<P>/hooks/` | `bootstrap.mjs`, `inject-contract.mjs`, `guard-spawn.mjs`, `guard-shell.mjs`, `guard-files.mjs`, `guard-phase.mjs`, `guard-net.mjs`, `guard-message.mjs` (`SendMessage` and the Task tools, every mode), `check-handback.mjs`, `check-stop.mjs`, `check-teammate.mjs`, `trajectory.mjs` with one `*.test.mjs` each |
| `<P>/probes/` | `probe-log.mjs` and `probe-hooks.json` (SL-0 only, never registered in a release); the released team probe uses the regular hooks, `run.mjs probe --team`, `checks/probe-check.mjs` and the agent `cdp6-core:probe` (§3.4 item 13); the probe record that gates anything is `<data>/probes/<claude-version>.json`, written only by `probe-check.mjs` (§3.4 item 14) |
| `<P>/lib/` | `contract.mjs`, `claims.mjs`, `data-dir.mjs`, `agent-name.mjs`, `grants.mjs`, `run-store.mjs` (lock, journal, single writer of `run.json`), `counters.mjs`, `capture.mjs` (the one writing module of `counters.json` and of `verdicts/`), `brief.mjs` (brief and message header parser), `registry.mjs` (checker registry with pins), `run-argv.mjs` (the one argv parser with its token charset, the call-shape rule and the subcommand-and-flag table of `run.mjs`, used by `run.mjs` and `guard-shell.mjs`, UC-11 step 1.2), `hook-key.mjs` (creation and reading of the host key, the keyed `--hook-token` and the nonce record, used by `bootstrap.mjs`, `guard-shell.mjs` and `run.mjs`, UC-11 step 1.3); `hooks/guard-vectors.json` (shared parity vectors) |
| `<P>/scripts/` | `run.mjs` (also the grant and worktree-registry commands, CH-48), `integrate.mjs`, `activity-check.mjs`, `egress-probe.mjs`, `worktree-setup.mjs`, `generate-contract.mjs`, `generate-manifest.mjs` |
| `<P>/checks/` | `idea-check.mjs`, `sources-check.mjs`, `trace-check.mjs`, `decision-check.mjs`, `plan-check.mjs`, `brief-lint.mjs`, `verdict-check.mjs`, `tdd-order.mjs`, `relax-check.mjs`, `loop-check.mjs`, `ban-check.mjs`, `probe-check.mjs`, `enforcement-check.mjs`, `size-check.mjs`, `release-check.mjs`, `registry.json` |
| `<P>/skills/` | `orchestrate`, `worker-contract`, `idea`, `explore`, `concept`, `refine`, `implement`, `verify`, `retro`, `loop-back`, `cdp6-doctor`, `cdp6-reference` |
| `<P>/agents/` | 13 role files (§2) and `probe.md` (Read and Bash, used only by the team probe) |
| `<P>/reference/cdp6-doctrine.md` | the CDP6 working-path doctrine (§9 budget) |
| `<P>/enforcement.json` | the enforcement-status table of §7 in machine-readable form |
| `<P>/manifest.json` | sha256 of every hook, lib, check, skill and agent file, generated at release |
| `<P>/evals/` | pressure-test cases (§8.4) |
| `.github/workflows/cdp6.yml` | CI (§8.2) |

### 1.3 Copy, reference or replace, per CDP5 part

| CDP5 part (path) | CDP6 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Worker contract: `cdp5-agents/skills/worker-contract/SKILL.md`, `core.md`, `lib/contract.mjs`, `scripts/generate-contract.mjs`, `hooks/inject-contract.mjs` | copy and extend as `CDP6-CONTRACT v1` (own header, own hash) | the contract changes (O1, O2, O6, O9, C11, C12 of §7); a shared contract would change CDP5 behaviour |
| `hooks/check-ack.mjs`, `hooks/check-handback.mjs`, `lib/claims.mjs`, `lib/handback.mjs` | copy and extend as `check-stop.mjs`, `check-handback.mjs` | gain verdict capture, written-file check, PII check (O1, O15, O16); the fail-open branch of `check-handback.mjs:54-61` (contract not verifiable → handback passes) is not copied: CDP6 denies (AC-12.11) |
| `hooks/guard-bash.mjs` (690 lines) | copy as `guard-shell.mjs` and extend | gains CDP6 state paths, the trajectory deny (O17), the human `ask` on `run.mjs approve`, the `permission_mode` check (TM-3) and a deny of `curl`/`wget` GET for writers (CDP5 lets GET through, `guard-bash.mjs:452-455`); a parity test keeps both guards' shared verdicts equal (CH-3) |
| `hooks/guard-spawn.mjs` | copy and extend | gains brief header, `brief-lint`, counters, base diff (O7, O14), phase role map |
| `lib/grants.mjs`, `scripts/grant-run.mjs`, `scripts/worktree-setup.mjs` | copy `lib/grants.mjs` and `worktree-setup.mjs`; the grant command of `grant-run.mjs` becomes the owner command `run.mjs grant`, which gains the refusal of a root with uncommitted changes; `worktree-setup.mjs` keeps setup and baseline and loses `--grant` (CH-48) | E2 costliest rule 1; grants live in `<data>/grants.json` (`grants.mjs:20-21`), which only the one excluded `run.mjs` pattern may write (§5.6 items 5, 6; r4-DA N1) |
| `lib/data-dir.mjs`, `lib/agent-name.mjs` | copy with CDP6 names | own data directory, own namespace |
| `hooks/bootstrap.mjs`, `skills/cdp5-doctor` | copy and extend as `bootstrap.mjs`, `cdp6-doctor` | gain run status re-injection, `synced copy` and drift detection (O5, O18) |
| `skills/orchestrate/SKILL.md` | replace by `cdp6-core:orchestrate` plus eight phase skills | CDP5 has no phases (doctrine §0.2) |
| Agents `devils-advocate`, `pessimistic-realist`, `adversarial-auditor`, `review-verify`, `review-code`, `konzept-reviewer`, `threat-modeler`, `impl-uc`, `impl-debug`, `impl-refactor`, `pm-retro` | copy into `cdp6-core:` with the changes of §2 | CDP5 hooks match only `^(cdp5-agents\|domain-agents):` (`cdp5-agents/hooks/hooks.json:17,61`), so a CDP5 agent in a CDP6 run would get the CDP5 contract |
| Agent `mcp-security-reviewer`, `roles-dormant/design-agent-uc.md` | not carried | an MCP-bound capability in a CDP6 run is stop-list item STOP-2 (§6.2); the human may run the CDP5 reviewer outside the run |
| `cdp5-gates` T1 skills `konzept-lint`, `konzept-mapper`, `konzept-model-api`, `roadmap-drift`, `retro-memory-loop-check`, `ac-to-test-scaffold`, `secrets-scan`, `pii-scan`, `skill-lint`, `test-gap` and the policy `reife-rubrik.policy.json` | reference (run from the installed `cdp5-gates`, pinned in `checks/registry.json` to the exact version `0.8.8` (marketplace.json at 74d17c8) and the sha256 of each referenced script, of every module it imports (`lib/args.mjs`, `lib/scan.mjs`; `konzept-lint.mjs:11` imports `../../lib/args.mjs`) and of `policy/reife-rubrik.policy.json`; any other version or hash is `checker-version`, fail closed; a new pin is a registry change with its own review) | deterministic, project-independent, no workflow semantics; copying 10 programs doubles their maintenance (CH-2) |
| Doctrine `konzept-design-pattern-v6.md` | reference for authoring (§2–§7, §10.1–§10.5, §11, §14, §23, §27, §28, §33, Part 5); replace for the working path (§0.2, §29, §32) by `reference/cdp6-doctrine.md` | authoring rules are unchanged; the working path is what CDP6 changes (CH-23) |
| `cdp5-gates/skills/cdp5-reference` | copy the lookup script as `cdp6-reference`, pointed at the CDP6 doctrine | own doctrine, same retrieval |
| `domain-agents`, `orbit-workflow` | not carried; they coexist unchanged | outside the engineering cycle |
| Usage log `usage-log.jsonl` | copy of the format in the CDP6 data directory | read by `loop-check.mjs` (§3, UC-08) |

### 1.4 Coexistence on one host

1. Hook matchers by namespace: every CDP6 `SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop` and agent-scoped `PreToolUse` decision applies only to agent types starting with `cdp6-core:`. CDP5's agent hooks match only `cdp5-agents:` and `domain-agents:` (`cdp5-agents/hooks/hooks.json:17,61`; `lib/agent-name.mjs:1`), so neither framework injects its contract into the other's agents.
2. Caller classes. The main session is the caller without `agent_id` (hooks.md:741-745: `agent_id` identifies a subagent or in-process teammate). Main-session decisions of CDP6 hooks apply only while an active CDP6 run exists for the session, except the write deny on `<data>/` and `docs/cdp6/probes/` (§3.4 item 14), which applies in every session in which `cdp6-core` is enabled (r4-KR G-6). For the hooks a run is active in every status except `DONE` and, after P8 exited, `ABANDONED`, so the `HALT-*` and `PARKED` statuses and an `ABANDONED` run before its P8 exit keep every main-session deny; `guard-shell.mjs` applies the `run.mjs` rules of UC-09 step 1 and UC-11 steps 1, 1.2 and 1.3 to every `run.mjs` call in every session in which `cdp6-core` is enabled, run or not (r5-KR G-A; AC-09.13; the halt-status deny of `run.json` edits in AC-13.3, SL-4, cc-KR D-2). Run lookup (TM3-2; `cwd` follows `cd`, hooks.md:598, so a `git init sub; cd sub` would hide a run found by `cwd` alone): (a) by the hook input's `session_id` (common field, hooks.md:732), which `run.mjs init` and `run.mjs resume` bind to the run in `<data>/active-runs.json`; the value reaches `run.mjs` only as the `--session-id` argument that `guard-shell.mjs` appends from the hook input (UC-09 step 1), never from the model or from a host-wide session pointer as CDP5 keeps one (`cdp5-agents/lib/data-dir.mjs:52-57`); (b) for an input with `agent_id`, by the run whose `counters.json` binds that `agent_id` (UC-12 step 3); (c) otherwise by any active run whose run root or project root is a prefix of the `cwd` or of the tool's target path, the project root resolved with `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` so a linked worktree maps to the main checkout (TM-5). When `<data>/active-runs.json` is missing, the hooks scan the run roots of the project for a `run.json` with status `active` and deny with "pointer missing" (AC-10.6). Whether subagent and teammate inputs carry the binding session's `session_id` is probe A-20; (b) covers them if not. The unknown-caller rule (one rule, used by §3.4, UC-16 and OR-34): a caller with `agent_id` is known when its `agent_type` starts with `cdp6-core:`, or when its `agent_id` is in the `members` array of the session's team config with an agent type starting with `cdp6-core:` and a name on the run's roster (binding key; the array carries "each member's name and agent ID" and "whatever agent type the lead named", agent-teams.md:260; the file is `~/.claude/teams/session-<first eight characters of session_id>/config.json`, agent-teams.md:249-251). Every other caller with `agent_id` is unknown: while the project has an active run it is denied every tool except Read, Grep and Glob, which stay under the private-store and secret-path rules (CDP5's `agentName` returns null for such callers and its guard then applies main-session rules, `agent-name.mjs:6-9`, `guard-bash.mjs:650-654`; CDP6 does not inherit that).
3. Data directories are separate: CDP5 uses its `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` and `~/.cache/cdp5-agents` (`cdp5-agents/lib/data-dir.mjs`), CDP6 its own and `~/.cache/cdp6-core`.
4. Contract injection per namespace: `CDP6-CONTRACT v1` only into `cdp6-core:` agents and registered CDP6 teammates; `WORKER-CONTRACT v2` only into CDP5 agents. In an active run `guard-spawn.mjs` denies every spawn whose `subagent_type` does not start with `cdp6-core:`, with or without a CDP6 brief header (TM-6, AC-12.6).
5. Role texts: both bootstraps print a role text. CDP6 recommends one framework per project. Whether project-level `enabledPlugins` enables a plugin for one project only (A-6) is probed in SL-0 before CH-17 is decided. When both are enabled, the CDP6 bootstrap states that inside an active CDP6 run the `cdp6-core:orchestrate` skill applies and CDP5's `orchestrate` does not, and `cdp6-doctor` reports `warn mixed`.
6. CDP5's main-session grant `ask` matches `grant-run.mjs … grant` and `worktree-setup.mjs --grant` (`guard-bash.mjs:633-643`, `:652-653`); CDP6 grants go through `run.mjs grant` (CH-48), which that pattern does not match, so a CDP6 grant is confirmed once, by the CDP6 `ask`.
7. Hook precedence: when CDP5 and CDP6 PreToolUse hooks disagree, "precedence is `deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow`" (hooks.md:1965), so a CDP5 guard that fails open never loosens a CDP6 deny.
8. Blast radius of a guard that cannot start: with `"onFailure": "block"` a CDP6 guard whose script, lib or `node` is missing blocks every tool call its matcher covers, also in projects without a CDP6 run, because the script that would see "no run" does not run. Cost: while `cdp6-core` is enabled and broken, the catch-all registration of `guard-phase.mjs` (`"*"`, hooks.md:279) blocks every tool call on the host, Read, Grep and Glob included, and denies every permission request (hooks.md:949); a guard timeout blocks the call it fires on (hooks.md:946); this lasts until the plugin is repaired or disabled. Before SL-8 builds the catch-all registration, the blast radius is the tool set of the named matchers built so far (in stage 1: Agent, Task, Bash, PowerShell, Monitor, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit; UC-10 Route). Probe A-6 (stage 1, §11.3) decides whether enabling per project limits this to CDP6 projects (CH-17); until it passes, `cdp6-core` is enabled only in the probe worktree's `.claude/settings.local.json`, never at user level, so the build session and other projects stay unaffected; the bootstrap prints the disable command when it detects a missing hook file.
9. Version floor: `onFailure` needs Claude Code v2.1.295 or later (hooks.md:447, :499). `run.mjs init` and `cdp6-doctor` read `claude --version` and refuse below 2.1.295; the bootstrap prints the refusal.
10. Shell-running tools (cc2-TM F7-1): the sandbox limits "apply to Bash, PowerShell, and Monitor commands" (sandboxing.md:9, :660), and `excludedCommands` entries are checked "against each Bash and Monitor call" (sandboxing.md:317), so a Monitor call can run the excluded `run.mjs`. `guard-shell.mjs` is therefore registered on `Bash|PowerShell|Monitor`, and `guard-phase.mjs`'s named matchers include Monitor (UC-10 Route). A Claude Code release that adds a tool which runs shell commands must be added to these matchers in `hooks.json` before `cdp6-core` is used with that release: the A-19 row of each probe record lists the shell-running tools the release's sandbox documentation names and the tool names its PreToolUse inputs show, and `cdp6-doctor` fails when that list holds a tool the `guard-shell.mjs` matcher lacks (AC-14.7, SL-9). A tool the matcher misses still reaches no owner record, because `run.mjs` exits 2 without a valid keyed token from the hook (UC-11 step 1.3).

## 2. Roles overview

| Role | Scope | Description | Typical person |
|---|---|---|---|
| `owner` | global | Gives the assignment verbatim, sets the autonomy level and budgets, decides every stop-list item, every (b)/(c) verdict and every `user challenge` decision; confirms grants and human gates in the permission prompt; is a gate of its own at every level: bans developments and vetoes a phase exit, decision or integration (§6.5) | the project owner |
| `orchestrator` | run | Main session in multi-agent mode: enters and exits phases with `run.mjs`, writes briefs, spawns roles, verifies claims, records rulings; does not analyse, research or implement | Claude Code main session |
| `operator` | run | Main session in single-agent mode: runs a phase itself with the phase skill; the judgment part of each gate still goes to a counter-role in fresh context | Claude Code main session |
| `explorer` | task | New. Answers exploration questions from the repository and the sources the orchestrator placed in the run directory; read-only, no network | subagent |
| `author` | task | New. Writes drafts of idea, concept, decisions and plan files inside the run directory only | subagent |
| `impl-uc`, `impl-debug`, `impl-refactor` | task | Implementation workers in their own worktree, test-first, no commit | subagent |
| `review-verify` | task | Verifies claims against the source; refuses a target whose hash drifted (O15) | subagent |
| `review-code` | task | Quality review of a diff that adds a class or seam or changes more than 150 lines | subagent |
| `konzept-reviewer` | task | Judgment part of the concept gate, including builder questions (questions that would change the plan); outside reviewer in every phase with a `D-n` (C13) | subagent |
| `threat-modeler` | task | Security share of concept, refinement and verification | subagent |
| `devils-advocate` | task | Counter-role for choices, before any vote or decision on them (O3), with a premise list (O4) | subagent |
| `pessimistic-realist` | task | Counter-role for forecasts (estimates, budgets) | subagent |
| `adversarial-auditor` | task | Counter-role for artefacts (the integrated diff in P6) | subagent |
| `pm-retro` | task | Blameless retro with carry-over, Kaizen and fork balance | subagent |
| `team lead` | run | Main session in team mode: creates the team after the team gate (§3.4), assigns tasks by message with a message header, maps provisional ids, owns the run files; does not implement | Claude Code main session |
| `teammate` | task | A `cdp6-core:` role spawned with `name` into the team (in-process display mode only); same contract, tools and guard tier as the role as a subagent, plus `SendMessage` (agent-teams.md:276) | in-process teammate |
| `probe` | task | The one teammate of the scripted team probe (`run.mjs probe --team`, §3.4 item 13); Read and Bash only; answers the lead's two probe messages and stops; never part of a phase | in-process teammate |
| `cdp6-hooks` | host | System actor: the hooks and checkers of `cdp6-core` | Node programs |
| `CI` | host | System actor: the GitHub Actions job of §8.2 that runs the suites and `release-check.mjs` (UC-15) | GitHub Actions job |

Changes to the copied role texts: `devils-advocate` gains O3 and O4 (§10, rows O3, O4), and its pass is invalid with `fresh context: no` or `rejected alternative provided: no` (orchestrate SKILL.md:68); its step 8, which in CDP5 says "in the ADR" (`cdp5-agents/agents/devils-advocate.md:32`), reads: until the owner records acceptance or reasoned rejection per objection with `run.mjs object` (r4-KR G-11); `impl-uc` gains O9; `pm-retro` gains O10 and O11; `review-verify` gains O15; `konzept-reviewer` gains the builder questions (E1 concept mechanism); every role gains the second answer line `run: <run-id> task: <task-id>` (contract rule C11) and, as a teammate, the message header (§3.4). Every triad role keeps the header line `conflict of interest: none | <what is at stake>` (doctrine §32.24, C13); `konzept-reviewer` and the review roles `review-verify` and `review-code` gain the same line (owner decision §0 item 18), so their capture with `none` can clear a `conflicted` one (C13), and only when its brief carries `clears: <capture id>` and its output disposes of each conflicted item (§0 item 19 (2), §5.3). Every slice that builds a triad role text, a clearing role's C13 line, a triad trigger (`decision-check.mjs`, `verdict-check.mjs`, C9t, OR-10, OR-11), the contract or the doctrine names the owner or a non-conflicted `konzept-reviewer` as outside reviewer, and the ledger names which (orchestrate SKILL.md:70). This applies to SL-1, SL-2, SL-4 (C9 part of `verdict-check.mjs`), SL-5 (with the `konzept-reviewer` C13 line and the C13 clearing branch), SL-6 (per-slice `decision-check.mjs`, `review-code` with the C13 line), SL-7 (`review-verify` with the C13 line; its own slice review is a `review-verify`, r5-KR G-I, r5-DA X-3), SL-10 and SL-11 (r4-KR G-4); the ledger names the outside reviewer before the slice starts; the devils-advocate role file is built in SL-2, the pessimistic-realist and adversarial-auditor role files in SL-5.

## 3. Lifecycle, ids and the common phase card

### 3.1 Phases

| Code | Phase | UC | Skill | Directory in the `run directory` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | idea | UC-01 | `cdp6-core:idea` | `idea/` |
| P2 | exploration | UC-02 | `cdp6-core:explore` | `exploration/` |
| P3 | concept | UC-03 | `cdp6-core:concept` | `concept/` |
| P4 | refinement | UC-04 | `cdp6-core:refine` | `plan/`, `briefs/` |
| P5 | implementation | UC-05 | `cdp6-core:implement` | `reports/`, `diffs/` |
| P6 | tests and verification | UC-06 | `cdp6-core:verify` | `verification/` |
| P7 | retro | UC-07 | `cdp6-core:retro` | `retro/` |
| P8 | loop back | UC-08 | `cdp6-core:loop-back` | `loop-back/` |

The `run directory` `R` is `docs/cdp6/runs/<run-id>/` in own repositories and `~/.claude/projects/<project>/memory/cdp6/runs/<run-id>/` in company repositories (doctrine §28 class rule; CH-4). An own repository that names its own record place in its CLAUDE.md or README keeps the runs there (`run.mjs init --root <path>` checks that the path string occurs in one of the two files; DECISIONS.md D-19 C2); the company-repository rule wins. The class is set at `run.mjs init --class own|company` and recorded in `run.json`.

### 3.2 Frozen-id scheme

| Id | Meaning | Born in | Frozen at | Cited by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RUN-<yyyymmdd>-<slug>` | run | init | init | every file of the run |
| `I-n` | idea item: problem, goal, non-goal, constraint, success measure | P1 | P1 exit | `Q-n`, `AC-nn.m`, retro |
| `D-n` | decision (register row: decision, Source, status, decider); every status change is made by `run.mjs decision D-n <proposed\|decided\|superseded> [--decider <name>]`, which stamps an ISO 8601 time in `run.json`; the model writes no decision time; `decided` with a decider other than the executor role (`operator`, `orchestrator`, `team lead`) is an owner command (UC-11 step 1); a `vetoed` status is set only by `run.mjs veto` | any phase | when its status becomes `decided` | ACs, slices, briefs, retro |
| `R-n` | ruling: what, why, cost if wrong, decision class | any phase | at write | ledger, retro |
| `Q-n` | exploration question | P2 (candidates from P8) | P2 exit | `X-n` |
| `SRC-n` | source file (URL with fetch date, HTTP status and sha256, or repo path at commit) | P2 | at write | `X-n` |
| `X-n` | exploration finding | P2 | P2 exit | concept ACs, `D-n` |
| `UC-nn`, `AC-nn.m` | use case, acceptance criterion | P3 | P3 exit | slices, tests, `trace map` |
| `S-n` | slice | P4 | P4 exit | briefs, reports, diffs |
| `F-Pk-n` | finding of a review or counter-role in phase k; a finding carried into a slice, plan or concept return has a `class:` cell `intent`, `plan` or `assumption` | at capture | at capture | re-briefs (cite the failed row), returns, retro |
| `B-n` | owner ban: kind (`path`, `dependency`, `technology`, `capability`, `scope`, `option`), value, why; Source `user` | `run.mjs ban` (owner command) | at write; lifted only by `run.mjs ban --lift B-n` (owner command), never edited | every checker of §6.5, the next run (`init --from` carries open bans) |
| `V-n` | owner veto on one target (`exit:<phase>`, `D-n`, `S-n`) | `run.mjs veto` (owner command) | at write | `run.mjs`, `decision-check.mjs`, `integrate.mjs`, retro |
| `K-n`, `L-n` | Kaizen action, lesson | P7 | P7 exit | P8, the next run's `Q-n` |
| `H-n` | halt with its stop code | any phase | at write | retro |
| `<agent-id>:<kind>-n` | provisional id of a teammate (team mode) | at message | mapped to a run id by the lead under the lock (`run.mjs id --map`) | nothing frozen may cite it unmapped |

Rule of the scheme: an id is never renumbered or reused. At a phase exit `run.mjs` records the sha256 of every artefact of the phase; a later change of a frozen artefact is drift, found by `trace-check.mjs` at every later gate (AC-03.4). A changed meaning gets a new id and a `supersedes: <old id>` cell. A frozen phase is reopened inside its run only by a return: the slice return of UC-05 step 6.2, the plan return of UC-04 step 6 or the concept return of UC-03 step 7; `run.mjs return` opens a new cycle `c` of P2 and of the phase the P2 exit names (P3 or P4); the frozen artefacts of cycle `c-1` stay on disk with their hashes; cycle `c` (from 2 on) writes its versions under `R/<phase directory>/cycle-<c>/` with the same file names (cycle 1 uses the phase directory itself), changed ids carry `supersedes:` cells, `trace-check.mjs` reads the highest cycle as current, and it fails any change of a cycle `c-1` file against its recorded exit hash as drift (AC-03.11); a returned P3 or P4 has no exit in its cycle, so for its files the hashes that `run.mjs return` wrote into `returns/concept.md` or `returns/plan.md` count as the recorded hashes (r4-DA CH-47 note). Every other change goes into a follow-up run that P8 prepares, which imports the artefact and supersedes the ids (CH-15). `trace-check.mjs` fails on an unmapped provisional id in any artefact at a gate (AC-12.13).

### 3.3 The common phase card

Every phase UC states the same eight items, so a phase is closed in itself:

1. Entry condition (what `run.mjs enter` checks).
2. Exit condition (what `run.mjs exit` checks).
3. Artefacts with exact paths and the ids they carry.
4. Executor in single-agent mode (operator with the phase skill), in multi-agent mode (orchestrator plus spoke roles) and in team mode (team lead plus teammates: members, who writes which shared file, how results meet, who is kept off judging their own work).
5. Gate: deterministic part (named checker) and judgment part (named counter-role, `captured verdict` only).
6. Anti-drift at handoff: the next phase re-reads the frozen artefacts from disk (hash compared) and cites ids, never a summary.
7. Standalone run: `run.mjs init --start <phase> --import <file>…` records the given files as an `imported` predecessor exit with their hashes and the owner as decider (permission prompt), so the phase runs alone with its inputs given as files.
8. Human gate per autonomy level (§6.1): at the levels of §6.1 that gate the phase, `run.mjs exit <phase>` leaves the phase `gate-pending` until `run.mjs approve <phase>` (owner command); an owner veto (§6.5) stops the exit at every level.

### 3.4 Three modes: single, multi, team

The mode is set per run (`run.mjs init --mode single|multi|team`); each phase card says which modes it allows. In a run of mode `team`, a phase whose card does not allow team mode (P1, P6, P7, P8) runs in mode `multi` with the team lead as orchestrator; `run.mjs enter` records the phase mode in `run.json` (r3-KR N6). All three enforce the same contract, counters, captures and phase gates (ledger 0021 G1b). On owner question 5: speed comes from independent work running in parallel; conversation pays where members must challenge or align with each other. The documentation says teams "work best when teammates can operate independently. For sequential tasks, same-file edits, or work with many dependencies, a single session or subagents are more effective" (agent-teams.md:26), and token use "scales with the number of active teammates" (agent-teams.md:326).

Facts team mode rests on (raw docs; items marked "reported" come from the research summary `r1-team-facts.md` and are confirmed in SL-0):

- `SubagentStart` runs "each time an in-process agent team teammate handles a new message" (hooks.md:2541); it can inject context. Split-pane teammates are not named there, so CDP6 allows the in-process display mode only (`teammateMode`, agent-teams.md:105-113; the default is in-process).
- The truemesh `CONTRACT-MISSING` of a named spawn (DECISIONS.md D-19 (1)) had three causes not separated: the hook did not fire, it fired with an `agent_type` the matcher missed, or the run used the synced 0.5.1 copy (facts F2). D-19 C5 says only that no teammate-specific start event exists; that is not a contradiction of hooks.md:2541. Probe A-11 separates the causes.
- `agent_id` identifies an in-process teammate in every hook input (hooks.md:741-745); whether `agent_type` carries the plugin-scoped role for a teammate is not documented (A-16).
- `TeammateIdle`: no matcher, fires on every occurrence, input `teammate_name` and `agent_id` (v2.1.290 or later, may be absent); exit 2 sends the teammate back to work with the stderr text; `{"continue": false}` stops it (hooks.md:2856-2890). `TaskCompleted`: exit 2 blocks the completion (hooks.md:2667-2701).
- Mailboxes are JSON files at `~/.claude/teams/{team-name}/inboxes/{agent-name}.json` (agent-teams.md:243); the team name is `session-` plus the first eight characters of the session id (agent-teams.md:249), so a hook derives it from `session_id`, not from the deprecated `team_name` field (hooks.md:2881). Whether a PreToolUse hook on `SendMessage` sees recipient and content is not documented (A-14).
- The team config `members` array carries each member's name, agent id and the agent type the lead named (agent-teams.md:260); it is the binding key of §1.4 item 2.
- A receiving agent is told a message came from another Claude session; a teammate cannot approve a permission prompt for the owner (agent-teams.md:302), and teammate permission prompts appear in the lead session (agent-teams.md:298). Teammate tools are the role's `tools` plus `SendMessage`, and in a session with the Task tools also `TaskCreate`, `TaskGet`, `TaskList`, `TaskUpdate` (agent-teams.md:276).
- `SendMessage` "doesn't require agent teams to be enabled" and resumes a completed subagent "without a new `Agent` invocation" (sub-agents.md:1135, :1147): outside team mode it bypasses the spawn counters.
- `onFailure` has no effect on `Stop`, `SubagentStop`, `TaskCompleted` and `TeammateIdle` (hooks.md:951-953): `check-stop.mjs` and `check-teammate.mjs` stay fail-open, so their effects are reconciled at phase exit (item 11).
- Teammates need an interactive session; with `-p` "a subagent that Claude names runs as an ordinary subagent" (agent-teams.md:60). Reported: `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1`; one team per session; no resumption of in-process teammates.

Team-mode mechanics (all ACs in UC-16):

1. Team gate (OR-1): before the first named spawn, `run.mjs team --phase <p> --members <role>:<name>,… --why "<why conversation instead of parallel spoke workers>" --messages <budget per wave>` records the roster and the answer in the ledger, after the readiness check of item 13 passed; it is an owner command (UC-11 step 1). `guard-spawn.mjs` denies a named spawn without a team gate for the entered phase, with a name outside the roster, with a role the phase card does not allow in team mode, or while `teammateMode` resolves to split panes.
2. Contract: `inject-contract.mjs` runs on every `SubagentStart` (catch-all matcher, filtered in the script) and injects `CDP6-CONTRACT v1` when the caller is known by the rule of §1.4 item 2 (prefix, or `agent_id` in the team config `members` array with a roster name). The binding is read from the team config, not inferred from start order.
3. Message header (C11): every `SendMessage` text opens with `msg: <run-id> <task-id> <kind> round <n>`, kind one of `brief`, `rebrief`, `question`, `answer`, `finding`, `verdict`, `vote`, `status`; a `verdict` also carries `target: <path>@<sha256>`.
4. Message log `R/team-log.jsonl` (`single writer`). `guard-message.mjs` runs on PreToolUse `SendMessage|TaskCreate|TaskGet|TaskList|TaskUpdate` in every mode of an active run. In modes `single` and `multi` it denies `SendMessage` (resume and cross-session messages bypass the spawn counters); the deny needs only `tool_name`. One exception, the O2 status message (§5.5): when probe A-14 shows recipient and text, it allows one message per captured handback whose header kind is `status` to the worker of that handback, records it in `counters.json` as a counted resume (not a re-brief round), and treats the worker's next handback as a new capture; a second status message on the same handback is denied; without A-14 there is no exception and §5.5 drops the message step (AC-12.22). In every mode it denies the four Task tools, so tasks reach agents only by brief or `rebrief` message; this also takes the session's own task list from the main session in single mode (the Task tools are a session feature, hooks.md:2614), a cost accepted because `run.json`, the plan and the ledger carry the run's work list (r3-DA O-14). In mode `team` it denies a recipient that is not a roster name. Primary path, if A-14 holds: `guard-message.mjs` checks the header, logs the message, counts `rebrief` rounds per task (at most two, then the slice return of UC-05 step 6.2), counts messages per team and wave (budget used up → yellow team fault, item 12), detects an oscillation (two members exchanging more than two rounds on one task without a new `finding` → yellow team fault), and captures `verdict` messages into `R/verdicts/` with hash. Fallback path, if A-14 fails: `check-teammate.mjs` on `TeammateIdle` copies new mailbox entries into the log (A-17: mailbox readable before it is consumed) and returns exit 2 with the reason on a violation; the checks then act after the message, not before it. Its stderr names the rule and the message hash, never the message text.
5. Provisional ids: teammates number their own findings `<agent-id>:F-n`; the lead maps them to run ids with `run.mjs id --map` under the run lock; `trace-check.mjs` fails on unmapped ids.
6. One author per shared document: the brief header line `writes: <path>` names the only files an agent may write; `guard-files.mjs` denies every other write in every mode, so two teammates never edit `konzept.md` or `plan.md` together.
7. Fresh context in a team (C9t): a counter-role that exchanged any message with the author of its target counts as co-author; `verdict-check.mjs` rejects its capture. Counter-roles therefore run as spoke subagents also in team mode, unless the team log shows no exchange with the author. A gate's `threat-modeler` is a separate spoke instance with no team-log exchange with the author, also where a `threat-modeler` teammate answered the author's questions (P3, P4).
8. Phase exit (OR-12): `run.mjs exit` refuses while a roster member has sent or received a message after its last `TeammateIdle`, or has not been stopped.
9. Votes (O3): a `vote` message on a `D-n` before its devils-advocate capture fails `decision-check.mjs`. In P5 a `question` or `answer` between implementers may not settle an interface choice that the brief does not fix: such a choice is a `D-n` proposal returned to the lead (contract C6b); `review-verify` checks the team log for settled choices (J, OR-35).
10. Session end: a run resumed in a new session marks the roster `lost`; open tasks are re-briefed by fresh spawn and counted.
11. Reconciliation at phase exit (fail-open events, hooks.md:951-953): `run.mjs exit` compares the run files and fails the exit with the gap named when any of these does not match: every spawn in `counters.json` has a binding and a capture or a `check-stop.mjs` line; every `tool_use_id` (PreToolUse input, hooks.md:1754) a guard allowed and logged in `hooks-fired.jsonl` has a `tool-log.jsonl` line (a PostToolUse failure leaves a gap that C9 would miss; a call declined in the permission prompt is a listed gap, cleared only by evidence the model cannot write, or by `run.mjs rule --human`, never by an executor ruling; r3-DA O-7). The evidence (r4-DA N6): a `hooks-fired.jsonl` line of the `PermissionRequest` observer for the same tool name and input hash (the PermissionRequest input has no `tool_use_id`, hooks.md:2085; it fires when a prompt is about to be shown, hooks.md:2089, so it proves a prompt, not a refusal) together with no PostToolUse or PostToolUseFailure line for that `tool_use_id` in `hooks-fired.jsonl`; in mode `auto`, a `PermissionDenied` observer line with that `tool_use_id` (hooks.md:2400, :2406) instead. `run.mjs` lists every cleared gap with its evidence in the ledger and in the `ask` reason of the next `run.mjs approve`, or in the final summary when no human gate follows. In team mode the exit also requires a `team-log.jsonl` line for every mailbox entry of a roster member (A-17 path) or for every `guard-message.mjs` line, and a `check-teammate.mjs` line for every idle roster member. A gap is STOP-8 in modes `single` and `multi` and a team fault in mode `team`.
12. Team check and switch to multi mode (Q-2 confirmed, T1 to T3, §0 item 14). The hook that sees a failed check records a `team-fault` line (level, check, agent id, evidence hash) in `counters.json`.
   12.1 Red, switch at once: a teammate's first answer lacks the contract acknowledgement (`check-teammate.mjs` at its first `TeammateIdle`); a teammate tool call not seen by the guards (a `tool-log.jsonl` line without the PreToolUse guard line of the same `tool_use_id`; the catch-all registration of `guard-phase.mjs` writes that line for every tool, so a tool no named guard matches raises no false red fault, r3-DA O-10); an unknown caller (§1.4 item 2); a message or a task change missing from the team log (a mailbox entry without a `team-log.jsonl` line, a changed task list under `~/.claude/tasks/session-<eight characters>/`, agent-teams.md:252, or any `TaskCompleted` event, since the Task tools are denied); a reconciliation mismatch at idle or at phase exit (item 11). From a red fault every CDP6 PreToolUse guard denies every tool call of a roster member except its shutdown response (r3-KR N7), `guard-message.mjs` denies every message except the lead's `shutdown_request` to roster members (sub-agents.md:1135) and the members' shutdown responses, `check-teammate.mjs` answers each teammate's next `TeammateIdle` with `{"continue": false}`, and `guard-phase.mjs` denies every lead tool call except Read, the lead's `shutdown_request` `SendMessage` to a roster member, `run.mjs mode multi` and `run.mjs halt` until the switch is recorded (r4-KR G-9). `run.mjs halt` under this lock sets `HALT-STOP`, so a teammate that rejects the shutdown (agent-teams.md:210) cannot leave an unattended run hanging (r3-TM TM3-7).
   12.2 Yellow, switch at the next turn: the wave's message budget is used up, or two teammates oscillate (item 4). The message that crosses the limit is denied; teammates finish their current turn without new `brief`, `rebrief` or `question` messages; when every roster member is idle, the lead's next action is the switch (the same lead tool-call deny as red, with its `run.mjs halt` exception, applies from then).
   12.3 The switch: the lead sends every teammate a shutdown request and runs `run.mjs mode multi --phase <p>`, which refuses while a roster member has not stopped, records a ruling `R-n` (fault, level, evidence), marks the roster `stopped`, and lets open tasks go to fresh spoke spawns recorded with `cause: team-fault` (not counted as re-brief rounds).
   12.4 Team work before the switch (T2): `run.mjs mode multi` writes `R/<phase directory>/team-handover.md` with the paths and sha256 of every file teammates wrote, the team log and the unmapped provisional ids, marked `unverified`. The first multi-mode worker's brief cites it as `material:`, never as a source; `run.mjs id --map` refuses to map a provisional id from a switched team until a `review-verify` capture on the handover names it as confirmed, and `trace-check.mjs` fails an artefact that cites an unmapped one (AC-12.13).
   12.5 No new team in the same phase and cycle after a switch (T3): `run.mjs team --phase <p>` refuses with the ruling id. The next phase may create a team after its own readiness check.
   No team rule falls back to judgment only.
13. Readiness check before every team creation (T4): `init --mode team` and `run.mjs team` refuse unless (a) `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1` is set in the environment the command inherits, (b) the session is interactive, shown by the owner's answered `ask` on `run.mjs team` itself (a `-p` session denies an `ask` it cannot show, hooks.md:1961; teammates need an interactive session, agent-teams.md:60), (c) `claude --version` meets the floor of §1.4 item 9, and (d) the probe record `<data>/probes/<claude-version>.json` for exactly this Claude Code version (item 14) shows A-11, A-16 and (A-14 or A-17) passed. A failed team probe switches team mode off for that version. After an update `bootstrap.mjs` sees a version without a record and prints that team mode is off; at the next team request the orchestrate skill has the lead run the scripted team probe automatically: `run.mjs probe --team` names one `cdp6-core:probe` teammate, two messages and one idle; while it holds an open probe entry in `counters.json`, `guard-spawn.mjs` and `guard-phase.mjs` allow that one named `cdp6-core:probe` spawn and `guard-message.mjs` allows two lead messages to it (r3-KR N4); `checks/probe-check.mjs` evaluates the `hooks-fired.jsonl` lines the CDP6 hooks wrote for it against the same expected records as SL-0 for A-11, A-16 and (A-14 or A-17) (§11.1) and writes a candidate. The candidate takes effect only after the owner runs `run.mjs probe --accept` on it (r3-DA O-9; orchestrator proposal answering r3-KR question 2, the owner may overrule).
14. Probe records (r3-KR N3, r3-TM TM3-5): `probe-check.mjs` is the only writer of `<data>/probes/<claude-version>.json`. An SL-0 record (`docs/cdp6/probes/<claude-version>.md`, human-readable with the raw log lines) and a team-probe candidate enter it only through the owner command `run.mjs probe --accept <file>` (UC-11 step 1, with the file's sha256 in the `ask`), which runs `probe-check.mjs --accept`; `run.mjs`, `guard-shell.mjs` and the readiness check read only that file. While no record is accepted for the running version, `guard-shell.mjs` treats `default` as the only prompting mode, so the first `run.mjs probe --accept` runs in `default`. `guard-files.mjs` denies writes to `<data>/` and `docs/cdp6/probes/` by every caller in every session in which `cdp6-core` is enabled, run or not, so a record cannot be planted before `run.mjs init` (r4-KR G-6), and writes to the `cdp6-core` and `cdp5-gates` plugin roots by every caller in a run; the sandbox's `denyWrite` covers `<data>/` (§5.6 item 5), which lies under the protected `~/.claude` anyway (sandboxing.md:543). `run.mjs probe --accept` and the accept part of `probe-check.mjs` are built in SL-8; until then no record is accepted, so `default` is the only prompting mode and `A3` is refused (AC-11.8). At `run.mjs exit` the sha256 of every own hook, lib, script and checker is compared with `manifest.json`; a difference is `checker-version`, fail closed (AC-11.13, AC-15.7).

Parallel work by phase and mode:

| Phase | Multi (spoke) | Team | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 idea | no | no | dialogue with the owner |
| P2 exploration | up to four `explorer` workers per wave | explorers that cross-check each other's findings | independent question groups; read-only |
| P3 concept, P4 refinement | the counter-roles of one gate in parallel | `author` with `threat-modeler` answering questions; counter-roles stay spoke (C9t) | read-only on the same frozen hash; one `author` per document |
| P5 implementation | one writer per slice | one writer per slice, messages for interface questions between parallel slices | no dependency path between the slices; disjoint `paths touched` (AC-04.6); one worktree each |
| P6 verification | `adversarial-auditor` and `review-verify` | no | different targets; C9t |
| P7 retro, P8 loop back | no | no | one author |

Token rule: fan-out costs about fifteen times the tokens of doing the work in one context (doctrine §32.10); a team adds context per message on top. Spoke fan-out needs `run.mjs fanout --wave <n> --why "<reason>"` before a second concurrent worker of a phase, a team needs the team gate (item 1); `guard-spawn.mjs` denies the spawn without either record (OR-1, AC-12.7, AC-16.1).

Consistency of shared ids and texts in every mode: ids come only from `run.mjs id <kind>` under the run lock (AC-09.5) or from the mapping of provisional ids (item 5); each shared document has one author (item 6); parallel results meet at the orchestrator or lead, findings merged by the one author, diffs by `integrate.mjs` in dependency order; texts are cited by id and hash, and a capture on a superseded hash is `stale` (O15).

If probes fail (T4): SL-11 is built only if A-11, A-16 and (A-14 or A-17) pass in SL-0 (rule A, r2-DA); otherwise team mode is not offered for that Claude Code version and every phase that would run as a team runs in multi mode, fully enforced. Unattended runs are interactive sessions the owner left running; in a `-p` session Claude Code denies an `ask` it cannot show (hooks.md:1961), so owner commands fail there, and a named spawn becomes an ordinary subagent (agent-teams.md:60), which the team checks report as a team fault.

## 4. Use cases

### UC-01: Run the idea phase (intake of an idea, feature request, issue or bug report)

**Actor:** `operator` (single), `orchestrator` with `author` (multi) or `team lead` (team); `owner` answers.
**Route:** skill `cdp6-core:idea`; `node <P>/scripts/run.mjs enter idea` … `run.mjs exit idea`

#### Phase card

| Item | Content |
|---|---|
| Entry | run initialised (`run.json` status `active`) with one `intake` file whose sha256 is recorded; the `intake` kind is one of `idea`, `feature-request`, `issue`, `bug-report`; or a P8 hand-over `R_prev/loop-back/next-run.md` cited by the init (kind `idea`, origin the previous run) |
| Exit | `R/idea/idea.md` passes `idea-check.mjs` and `decision-check.mjs`; every `D-n`, whatever its Source, has a `captured verdict` of `devils-advocate` stamped before its `decided` stamp, with verdict (a) or (a) with conditions, or an owner record per objection (`run.mjs object`, UC-11 step 1); the approach-choice `D-n` decided with decider `owner`; autonomy level and budgets set by the owner |
| Artefacts | `R/idea/intake.md`: kind, origin (tracker id or URL with fetch date and HTTP status, or `conversation` with date), text verbatim (data: never the objective); `R/idea/assignment.md`: the objective the owner confirmed (equal to the intake text only for kind `idea` with origin `conversation`; otherwise written separately and confirmed behind an `ask`, TM-7); `R/idea/idea.md`: assignment verbatim; `I-n` rows (problem, goals, non-goals, constraints, success measures); decision register `D-n` with the Source column `user`, `codebase`, `prior-artifact`, `delegated-default` (ST decision register); approach options with the null option; autonomy level `A0`–`A3`, token budget, loop budget |
| Single | operator with the idea skill interviews the owner (Clarify-First, doctrine §1.9); one question at a time; writes `idea.md` |
| Multi | orchestrator interviews the owner (dialogue is not delegated); `author` turns the transcript excerpt into `idea.md` |
| Team | not allowed: the phase is a dialogue with the owner |
| Gate | deterministic: `idea-check.mjs` (sections present, every `I-n` and `D-n` well-formed, Source values from the enum, no `TBD` or `NEEDS CLARIFICATION`, assignment hash equals `run.json`), `decision-check.mjs` (O3 order on the stamps); judgment: `devils-advocate` on the approach choice and the existence question (doctrine §27.1.1) with the null option "do not build" |
| Anti-drift | assignment stored once, by hash; every later brief carries it verbatim and `brief-lint.mjs` compares the hash |
| Standalone | `run.mjs init --start idea --assignment <file>` |
| Human gate | the approach choice is a `user challenge` at every level (gstack office-hours: a STOP even when one option clearly wins) |

#### Behavior

1. `run.mjs enter idea` checks the run is active and no phase is entered; it records `entered_at`.
   1.1 A tracker issue, feature request or bug report is fetched by the main session (workers have no network) and stored verbatim in `intake.md` with its origin; it then runs through the same steps as an idea.
   1.2 Kind-specific `I-n` rows: a `bug-report` adds observed behaviour, expected behaviour, reproduction steps and environment; a `feature-request` adds the requester and the stated value; an `issue` adds its tracker state at fetch time.
2. The executor asks the owner for problem, goals, non-goals, constraints and success measures; each answer becomes an `I-n` row.
   2.1 An answer that leaves two readings is asked again, not resolved by the executor.
   2.2 A default the executor proposes is a `D-n` with Source `delegated-default` until the owner confirms it (then Source `user`).
3. The executor lists the approach options with costs and the null option as `D-1` with status `proposed`; `devils-advocate` runs on it; then the owner picks, with the objections as input (`run.mjs decision D-1 decided --decider owner`, an owner command behind the `ask`).
4. The owner sets the `autonomy level` and budgets; `run.mjs set-autonomy` records them behind a permission `ask`.
   4.1 A constraint the owner wants enforced, not only stated, is recorded as a ban `B-n` with `run.mjs ban` (§6.5); the `I-n` constraint row cites it.
5. `run.mjs exit idea` runs the gate: `idea-check.mjs`, then the verdict lookup for `devils-advocate`.
   5.1 A missing checker or a checker exit 2 leaves the phase `gate-pending` (`fail closed`).
   5.2 Verdict (b), (c) or a `conflicted` capture not cleared by the C13 clearing capture blocks until the owner records a decision per objection with `run.mjs object <F-P1-n> accept|reject --why "<why>"` (STOP-5).
6. On pass `run.mjs` records the exit, the artefact hashes and the ledger line.

#### Error cases

| Case | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Assignment file missing or empty | `run.mjs init` exit 2, no run created |
| `intake` kind outside the enum, or origin missing | `run.mjs init` exit 2 with the field |
| Bug report without reproduction steps | `idea-check.mjs` fails with the missing row; the owner supplies them or the reporter is asked |
| Owner unreachable in an unattended run | terminal status `HALT-STOP` with STOP-9 (`user challenge` open) |
| `idea-check.mjs` missing from the plugin | gate result `checker-missing`, phase stays `gate-pending`, unattended run ends `HALT-CHECKER` |
| A `D-n` has Source `delegated-default` at exit | gate fails with the row id; the owner confirms or the executor reopens the question |
| Devils-advocate verdict (c) "do not build" | phase `blocked`; the owner accepts with `run.mjs abandon --why "<why>"` (run ends `ABANDONED`) or rejects per objection with `run.mjs object … reject --why "<why>"` |

#### Acceptance criteria

| # | Criterion | Test type | Test class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AC-01.1 | `run.mjs exit idea` with an `idea.md` lacking a non-goals section exits 1 and names the section | Unit | `checks/idea-check.test.mjs` "fires: missing non-goals" | red |
| AC-01.2 | A `D-n` row with Source `guess` fails `idea-check.mjs` with the row id | Unit | `checks/idea-check.test.mjs` "fires: unknown source enum" | red |
| AC-01.3 | With `idea-check.mjs` deleted, `run.mjs exit idea` exits 2 and `run.json` keeps state `gate-pending` | Integration | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: checker missing" | red |
| AC-01.4 | Exit without a `captured verdict` of `devils-advocate` for the approach choice is refused | Integration | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: verdict missing" | red |
| AC-01.5 | `run.mjs set-autonomy` returns a permission `ask` decision from `guard-shell.mjs` | Unit | `hooks/guard-shell.test.mjs` "fires: autonomy ask" | red |
| AC-01.12 | `run.mjs decision D-1 decided --decider owner` returns an `ask` from `guard-shell.mjs`, and is denied in a permission mode outside the A-5 record and for a caller with `agent_id` | Unit | `hooks/guard-shell.test.mjs` "fires: forged owner decider" | red |
| AC-01.6 | An assignment whose sha256 differs from `run.json` fails `idea-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/idea-check.test.mjs` "fires: assignment hash drift" | red |
| AC-01.7 | `run.mjs init --kind ticket` exits 2 naming the four allowed kinds | Unit | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: unknown intake kind" | red |
| AC-01.10 | `run.mjs init --kind issue` without a separate `--assignment` file exits 2 | Unit | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: intake as objective" | red |
| AC-01.11 | `D-1` stamped `decided` before its devils-advocate capture fails `decision-check.mjs` at the P1 gate | Unit | `checks/decision-check.test.mjs` "fires: owner pick before counter-role" | red |
| AC-01.8 | An `intake.md` of kind `issue` without origin fails `idea-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/idea-check.test.mjs` "fires: missing origin" | red |
| AC-01.9 | A `bug-report` intake without reproduction-step rows fails `idea-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/idea-check.test.mjs` "fires: bug without reproduction" | red |
| AC-01.13 | `idea-check.mjs` fails when the approach-choice `D-n` is `decided` with a decider other than `owner` | Unit | `checks/idea-check.test.mjs` "fires: approach chosen without owner" | red |

### UC-02: Run the exploration phase

**Actor:** `operator` (single), `orchestrator` with `explorer` and `review-verify` (multi), or `team lead` with `explorer` teammates (team).
**Route:** skill `cdp6-core:explore`; `run.mjs enter exploration` … `run.mjs exit exploration`

#### Phase card

| Item | Content |
|---|---|
| Entry | P1 exited (or imported); in a follow-up run the previous run's `loop-back/next-run.md` is an input; or a slice return (UC-05 step 6.2), plan return (UC-04 step 6) or concept return (UC-03 step 7) opened cycle `c` with `R/exploration/returns/S-n.md`, `returns/plan.md` or `returns/concept.md` as input |
| Exit | every `Q-n` answered by at least one `X-n` or marked `open` with owner; `sources-check.mjs` exit 0; `review-verify` pass on every `X-n` that an option or `D-n` rests on; in a return cycle every returned finding (`F-P5-n` of a slice, `F-P4-n` of the plan, `F-P3-n` of the concept) is answered by an `X-n` and `review-verify` passed its `class:` |
| Artefacts | `R/exploration/questions.md` (`Q-n`, each citing an `I-n`, a previous run's `K-n`/`L-n` or a returned `F-P5-n`); `R/exploration/answers-<name>.md` (team mode, one per explorer); `R/exploration/returns/S-n.md` (written by `run.mjs return`: the failed attempts' report and diff paths with sha256, their captured findings `F-P5-n` with `class:`, the triage target phase); `R/exploration/sources/SRC-n.md` (URL, fetch date, HTTP status, sha256 of the fetched text; or repo path at commit); `R/exploration/findings.md` (`X-n`: claim, `SRC-n` with locator, answered `Q-n`); `R/exploration/options.md` (approach options with costs and the null option); throwaway prototypes only inside a worktree, deleted at exit (doctrine §27.3) |
| Single | operator fetches sources, writes questions, findings and options |
| Multi | orchestrator fetches every web source into `sources/` (workers have no network; CH-11); up to four `explorer` workers per wave on independent question groups; `review-verify` on the findings an option rests on |
| Team | up to four `explorer` teammates, each with its own question group (`writes:` its own `exploration/answers-<name>.md`); they message `finding` and `question` to each other to cross-check; the lead maps provisional ids and is the one author of `findings.md` and `options.md`; `review-verify` runs as a spoke subagent (C9t) |
| Gate | deterministic: `sources-check.mjs` (every cited `SRC-n` exists and its sha256 matches; every source is cited at least once; every `X-n` cites a source; a source of type `context` is never the only evidence of an `X-n`; every `Q-n` answered or `open` with owner; every web `SRC-n` has a `tool-log.jsonl` `WebFetch` line by `main` with the same URL and its URL is in `sources.urls`, r3-TM TM3-6); judgment: `review-verify` |
| Anti-drift | the `research firewall`: project context shapes questions and never counts as evidence (BMAD deep-recon); findings cite source hashes, so a re-fetched page with other content is a new `SRC-n` |
| Standalone | `run.mjs init --start exploration --import idea.md` |
| Human gate | at `A0` |

#### Behavior

1. `run.mjs enter exploration` checks P1 `exited` or `imported`, or an open slice, plan or concept return.
2. The executor derives `Q-n` from the goals and constraints (and the previous run's open `K-n`/`L-n`).
   2.1 In a return cycle each returned finding (`F-P5-n` of a slice, `F-P4-n` of the plan, `F-P3-n` of the concept) becomes a `Q-n` by its class: `intent` asks the owner which reading holds, which is STOP-4: the run halts `HALT-INTENT-GAP` with the readings listed until the owner answers with `run.mjs rule --human`, and that ruling is the source (r3-DA O-12); `plan` asks what the slice cut or the estimate missed; `assumption` re-checks the code or host behaviour with a source or a probe. The exit names the next phase per class: `intent` and an `assumption` cited by an AC or `D-n` → P3; `plan` and other assumptions → P4. After a concept return the exit names P3 for every class, because P3 has no exit in the returned cycle.
3. Sources are fetched by the main session only, from URLs the owner registered with `run.mjs allow-url` (UC-13 step 3), and written as `SRC-n` files with hash.
   3.1 A fetch failure is recorded with its HTTP status, not retried more than twice.
4. Findings are written per question with source locators; options with costs and the null option are derived from them.
5. `run.mjs exit exploration` runs `sources-check.mjs` and looks up the `review-verify` verdicts.
   5.1 Bug-report route (CDP5 Mode B, doctrine §29): for kind `bug-report` the exploration is the reproduction (feedback loop §27.5, stale data ruled out first §32.8 (6)); a reproducing `X-n` carries the failing command and its output. `run.mjs exit exploration --route bug` then records P3 and P4 as `skipped` and writes one regression AC (`AC-00.n`, citing the reproducing `X-n`) and one slice with its brief, only when: the reproduction fails as reported; the fix fits one slice with one writer (size floor); no `D-n` with Source other than `user` is needed; nothing on the stop list is touched. Otherwise the full path runs. At `A0` the owner confirms the route.
   5.2 A report that does not reproduce: `run.mjs exit exploration --route not-reproducible` records P2 `exited` with the `X-n` carrying the non-reproducing commands and outputs, and P3, P4, P5 and P6 `skipped` with that route; P7 and P8 run as usual (P8 routes the finding, for example `defer` with a question to the reporter), and the run ends `DONE`.
6. On pass the exit and hashes are recorded.

#### Error cases

| Case | Behavior |
|---|---|
| An `X-n` cites only the project's own CONTEXT.md | `sources-check.mjs` fails with the id (`research firewall`) |
| A source file was edited after its hash was taken | hash mismatch, gate fails with the `SRC-n` |
| An explorer brief asks for a URL the brief does not carry | the explorer stops (contract rule 5) and returns the URL as a need |
| `review-verify` returns `widerlegt` for an `X-n` | the finding is struck or re-researched; the option that rests on it is re-derived |
| Question group too large for one worker (four workers per wave exceeded) | STOP-6 budget at `A3`; the orchestrator asks at `A0`–`A2` |
| A needed URL is not registered | `guard-net.mjs` denies the fetch; the URL is listed in `exploration/needs.md` and its `Q-n` stays `open` with owner until the owner registers it |
| A returned `F-P5-n`, `F-P4-n` or `F-P3-n` has no answering `X-n` | `sources-check.mjs` fails with the finding id |

#### Acceptance criteria

| # | Criterion | Test type | Test class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AC-02.1 | An `X-n` whose only source has type `context` fails `sources-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/sources-check.test.mjs` "fires: context-only evidence" | red |
| AC-02.2 | A `SRC-n` file changed after its hash was recorded fails with the source id | Unit | `checks/sources-check.test.mjs` "fires: source hash drift" | red |
| AC-02.3 | A source never cited by any `X-n` fails with the source id | Unit | `checks/sources-check.test.mjs` "fires: uncited source" | red |
| AC-02.4 | A `Q-n` without finding and without `open` owner fails with the question id | Unit | `checks/sources-check.test.mjs` "fires: unanswered question" | red |
| AC-02.5 | `run.mjs enter exploration` before P1 exit is denied by `guard-phase.mjs` and by `run.mjs` itself | Integration | `hooks/guard-phase.test.mjs` "fires: predecessor missing" | red |
| AC-02.6 | `exit exploration --route bug` on an intake of kind `feature-request` is refused | Unit | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: bug route for other kind" | red |
| AC-02.7 | `exit exploration --route bug` without a reproducing `X-n` carrying a failing exit status is refused | Unit | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: bug route without reproduction" | red |
| AC-02.8 | After the bug route, `run.json` holds P1 and P2 `exited` and P3 and P4 `skipped` with the route reason | Integration | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "records skipped phases" | red |
| AC-02.9 | After `--route not-reproducible`, `run.mjs enter retro` is allowed and `run.mjs enter implementation` is denied | Integration | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: implementation after not-reproducible" | red |
| AC-02.10 | In a return cycle, a returned `F-P5-n` without an answering `X-n` fails `sources-check.mjs` with the finding id | Unit | `checks/sources-check.test.mjs` "fires: returned finding unanswered" | red |
| AC-02.11 | A web `SRC-n` without a `tool-log.jsonl` `WebFetch` line by `main` for its URL, or whose URL is not in `sources.urls`, fails `sources-check.mjs` with the source id | Unit | `checks/sources-check.test.mjs` "fires: web source without fetch" | red |
| AC-02.12 | A returned finding of class `intent` sets the run `HALT-INTENT-GAP` with STOP-4 at the P2 entry of the return cycle | Unit | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: intent return without owner" | red |

### UC-03: Run the concept phase

**Actor:** `operator`, `orchestrator` or `team lead` with `author`, `konzept-reviewer`, `devils-advocate`, `threat-modeler`.
**Route:** skill `cdp6-core:concept`; `run.mjs enter concept` … `run.mjs exit concept`

#### Phase card

| Item | Content |
|---|---|
| Entry | P2 exited or imported (in a return cycle: the P2 exit names P3) |
| Exit | `konzept-lint` exit 0; `trace-check.mjs` and `decision-check.mjs` exit 0; every counter-role of the gate passes (`konzept-reviewer` score at or above the policy threshold, `threat-modeler` with security share) within at most five judgment rounds (owner decision §0 item 12), otherwise the concept return of step 7 (owner decision §0 item 17); `devils-advocate` verdict on every `D-n` of the concept before its status became `decided` |
| Artefacts | `R/concept/konzept.md` in the CDP5 concept structure (doctrine §2–§7, §11.7, §14, §23), UCs `UC-nn`, ACs `AC-nn.m`, each AC citing an `I-n`, `X-n` or `D-n`; `R/decisions.md` (all `D-n` of the run in ADR form: title, date, decider, Source, status, options with null option, choice, consequences, supersede link) |
| Single | operator writes concept and decisions with the concept skill |
| Multi | `author` writes; `devils-advocate` runs on each proposed `D-n` "before any vote or decision on a proposed choice" (O3); `konzept-reviewer` with builder questions; `threat-modeler` when the concept has a security share |
| Team | `author` teammate (`writes:` `konzept.md`, `decisions.md`) and a `threat-modeler` teammate that answers the author's questions by message; `devils-advocate`, `konzept-reviewer` and the gate's `threat-modeler` run as separate spoke subagents with no team-log exchange with the author, because a counter-role that messaged the author is a co-author (C9t) |
| Gate | deterministic: `konzept-lint` (cdp5-gates, referenced), `trace-check.mjs` (every AC cites an existing frozen id; every goal `I-n` is covered by at least one AC; no `NEEDS CLARIFICATION` marker; no AC or UC cites a banned scope item, §6.5), `decision-check.mjs` (every `decided` `D-n` has a `captured verdict` of `devils-advocate` whose capture stamp is earlier than the `decided` stamp written by `run.mjs decision`; no `vote` message on it before the capture; (b)/(c) objections each have an owner record with decider and stamp, counted only from `run.json` where `run.mjs object` writes it; no `decided` `D-n` selects a banned option or is vetoed); judgment: `konzept-reviewer` (and `threat-modeler` with security share) |
| Anti-drift | the concept cites ids; the next phase reads `konzept.md` and `decisions.md` from disk and `trace-check.mjs` compares their hashes with the exit record |
| Standalone | `run.mjs init --start concept --import idea.md --import findings.md` |
| Human gate | at `A0` and `A1` |

#### Behavior

1. `run.mjs enter concept` checks P2.
2. The executor writes UCs with numbered steps, error-case tables and AC tables; every AC cites its origin id.
3. Each choice among alternatives is written as a `D-n` with status `proposed`, its alternatives with costs and the null option.
   3.1 `devils-advocate` receives the `D-n`, the alternatives, and the code paths the choice touches (O4); its verdict is captured by the handback hook.
   3.2 Only after verdict (a) or (a) with conditions may the status become `decided`; under (b), (c) or a `conflicted` capture not cleared by the C13 clearing capture the owner records acceptance or reasoned rejection per objection with `run.mjs object`. A verdict "not decidable without <evidence>" is no pass: the executor supplies the evidence and re-runs `devils-advocate` as the next round on the same `D-n`; without it, STOP-4 (r4-KR G-10; the same holds at every gate with a `D-n`, UC-12 step 5).
4. `konzept-lint` runs; its findings are fixed before the judgment pass.
5. `konzept-reviewer` scores the concept and lists builder questions; the gate's counter-roles run in rounds, each round on the revised hash. The cycle ends at the first round in which every counter-role of the gate passes; after round 5 without that, the concept return of step 7 (owner decisions §0 items 12, 17; item 12 replaces doctrine §10.3 "max. 2 judgment rounds" for this gate). `counters.json` counts the rounds per gate.
6. `run.mjs exit concept` runs the checkers, the verdict lookup and records the exit.
7. Concept return (owner decision §0 item 17): when round 5 ends without every counter-role passing, the orchestrator types each open objection `F-P3-n` as `intent`, `plan` or `assumption` and runs `run.mjs return concept --class <class per finding>`. It records a ruling `R-n`, writes `R/exploration/returns/concept.md` (the unresolved objections with class, the `konzept.md` and `decisions.md` hashes), and opens cycle `c+1` of P2; the P2 exit names P3 (UC-02 step 2.1), and P3 of the new cycle starts its round count at one. Each gate brief of the new cycle lists the returned objections of its role, and that role's pass must dispose of each (§5.3, AC-03.15).
   7.1 Bound: one concept return per run. When round 5 of P3 ends again without every counter-role passing, `run.mjs return concept` refuses and the run halts with STOP-7 for the owner.

#### Error cases

| Case | Behavior |
|---|---|
| `cdp5-gates` not installed | `konzept-lint` resolves to nothing, gate `checker-missing`, `fail closed` |
| `cdp5-gates` version or script hash differs from the registry pin | gate refuses with `checker-version`, the found version and hash |
| A `D-n` became `decided` before its devils-advocate capture | `decision-check.mjs` fails with the id and both times (O3) |
| An AC cites an `X-n` that does not exist | `trace-check.mjs` fails with AC and cited id |
| Round 5 ends without every counter-role of the gate passing | concept return to P2 (step 7); after the one return used up: STOP-7 |
| A `D-n` selects an option the owner banned, or the owner vetoed it | `decision-check.mjs` fails with the `D-n` and the `B-n` or `V-n` |
| `idea.md` changed after P1 exit | `trace-check.mjs` reports `frozen-artefact-drift` with path and both hashes |

#### Acceptance criteria

| # | Criterion | Test type | Test class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AC-03.1 | A `decided` `D-n` whose devils-advocate capture is later than its decision time fails `decision-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/decision-check.test.mjs` "fires: vote before counter-role" | red |
| AC-03.2 | A (b) objection without a human record (decider and date) fails `decision-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/decision-check.test.mjs` "fires: block without human record" | red |
| AC-03.3 | A goal `I-n` covered by no AC fails `trace-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/trace-check.test.mjs` "fires: uncovered goal" | red |
| AC-03.4 | A frozen artefact edited after its phase exit fails `trace-check.mjs` with path and hashes | Unit | `checks/trace-check.test.mjs` "fires: frozen artefact drift" | red |
| AC-03.5 | With `cdp5-gates` absent the concept gate exits 2 and `run.json` stays `gate-pending` | Integration | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: referenced checker missing" | red |
| AC-03.6 | A sixth judgment-round spawn of a P3 gate counter-role in one cycle is denied; a fifth is allowed; a third review round of a gate outside P3 and P4 is denied | Unit | `hooks/guard-spawn.test.mjs` "fires: sixth concept round", "fires: third review round" | red |
| AC-03.8 | A `decided` `D-n` whose chosen option matches a `B-n` of kind `option`, or that has a `V-n`, fails `decision-check.mjs` with both ids | Unit | `checks/decision-check.test.mjs` "fires: banned option decided" | red |
| AC-03.9 | An AC citing an `I-n` or term named by a `B-n` of kind `scope` fails `trace-check.mjs` with both ids | Unit | `checks/trace-check.test.mjs` "fires: banned scope item" | red |
| AC-03.7 | A `konzept-lint` script whose sha256 differs from the registry pin yields `checker-version` and exit 2 | Unit | `lib/registry.test.mjs` "fires: pinned hash mismatch" | red |
| AC-03.10 | An objection record written into `decisions.md` by Edit, without a `run.mjs object` record in `run.json`, is not counted by `decision-check.mjs` | Unit | `checks/decision-check.test.mjs` "fires: objection record outside run.json" | red |
| AC-03.11 | In cycle 2 of a return, a change of the cycle-1 `konzept.md` fails `trace-check.mjs` with path and both hashes, while `concept/cycle-2/konzept.md` is read as current | Unit | `checks/trace-check.test.mjs` "fires: cycle c-1 edit in return cycle" | red |
| AC-03.12 | An AC citing an `X-n` that does not exist fails `trace-check.mjs` with AC and cited id | Unit | `checks/trace-check.test.mjs` "fires: cited id missing" | red |
| AC-03.13 | `run.mjs return concept` after round 5 with an open `konzept-reviewer` block writes `returns/concept.md`, opens P2 cycle 2, and `run.mjs enter refinement` is denied until P3 of cycle 2 exits | Integration | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: refinement before concept return cycle exits" | red |
| AC-03.14 | A second `run.mjs return concept` in one run is refused and the run status becomes `HALT-ROUNDS` with STOP-7 | Unit | `scripts/run.test.mjs` "fires: second concept return" | red |
| AC-03.15 | In cycle 2 after a concept return, a P3 gate brief for `threat-modeler` that omits a returned `F-P3-n` of that role, and in cycle 2 after a plan return a P4 gate brief for `pessimistic-realist` that omits a returned `F-P4-n`, fail `brief-lint.mjs` with the finding id; a pass capture without a `disposition:` line per returned id, with a `resolved` or `withdrawn` disposition that cites neither an id or `file:line` of a cycle-2 artefact nor an owner record in `run.json`, or with an `upheld` disposition is rejected by `verdict-check.mjs` (the `upheld` one counted as `block`) | Unit | `checks/brief-lint.test.mjs` "fires: return-cycle gate brief omits returned objection", "fires: plan-return gate brief omits returned objection"; `checks/verdict-check.test.mjs` "fires: pass without disposition of returned objection", "fires: resolved disposition without cycle c+1 citation", "fires: upheld disposition counted as pass" | red |

### UC-04: Run the refinement phase

**Actor:** `operator`, `orchestrator` or `team lead` with `author`, `devils-advocate`, `pessimistic-realist`, `threat-modeler`.
**Route:** skill `cdp6-core:refine`; `run.mjs enter refinement` … `run.mjs exit refinement`

#### Phase card

| Item | Content |
|---|---|
| Entry | P3 exited or imported (in a return cycle: P3 of the cycle exited, or the P2 exit names P4) |
| Exit | `plan-check.mjs`, `brief-lint.mjs` (every brief), `trace-check.mjs` and `decision-check.mjs` exit 0 (bans included, §6.5); `devils-advocate` on the plan's choices before they are stamped `decided`; `pessimistic-realist` on the estimate; every counter-role of the gate passes within at most five rounds (owner decision §0 item 15), otherwise the plan return of step 6 |
| Artefacts | `R/plan/plan.md` (slices `S-n`: title, HITL or AFK, dependencies, cited `AC-nn.m`, paths touched, gate commands, `input-class block` or `input-classes: n/a <reason>`, status); `R/plan/estimate.md` (ranges with open assumptions); `R/briefs/S-n.md` (one brief per slice, §5.3 header) |
| Single | operator writes plan, estimate and briefs |
| Multi | `author` writes; `devils-advocate` on slice cut and new subsystems; `pessimistic-realist` on the estimate with the threshold as a quantity; `threat-modeler` with security share |
| Team | as UC-03: one `author` teammate writes plan, estimate and briefs; counter-roles, the gate's `threat-modeler` included, stay separate spoke subagents (C9t) |
| Gate | deterministic: `plan-check.mjs` (every AC of the concept is in exactly one slice; each slice AC names an observable outcome and a test name; dependency graph acyclic; slices without a dependency path have disjoint `paths touched`; no `paths touched` under a banned path; no `TBD`; AFK only when the decidable AFK conditions of doctrine §28.3 hold), `brief-lint.mjs` (§5.3; no banned scope item in objective or scope block), `trace-check.mjs`, `decision-check.mjs`; judgment: `devils-advocate`, `pessimistic-realist` |
| Anti-drift | each brief carries pointers and the assignment verbatim, never a summary of the concept; the plan is re-read from disk before acting on approval (BMAD) |
| Standalone | `run.mjs init --start refinement --import konzept.md --import decisions.md` |
| Human gate | at `A0`, `A1`, `A2` (plan approval) |

#### Behavior

1. `run.mjs enter refinement` checks P3.
2. The executor cuts vertical slices (doctrine §27.2), each with ACs, paths and gate commands.
   2.1 A slice whose code reads or validates input, writes records or loops over items carries the `input-class block` (O6).
3. One brief per slice is written; `brief-lint.mjs` runs on each.
4. The estimate is written as ranges; `pessimistic-realist` judges it.
   4.1 The gate's counter-roles (`devils-advocate`, `pessimistic-realist`, `threat-modeler` with security share) run in rounds on the revised hash, at most five; the cycle ends at the first round in which every counter-role passes (owner decision §0 item 15). `counters.json` counts the rounds per gate.
5. `run.mjs exit refinement` runs the checkers and the verdict lookup; at `A0`–`A2` the exit waits for `run.mjs approve refinement` (permission `ask`).
6. Plan return (owner decision §0 item 15): when round 5 ends without every counter-role passing, the orchestrator types each open objection `F-P4-n` as `intent`, `plan` or `assumption` and runs `run.mjs return plan --class <class per finding>`.
