### 11.2 Forecast from a run model

Run model per slice (`pessimistic-realist` rounds 1 to 4): first delivery = implementer 1 + `review-verify` 1 + `review-code` 1 when the slice adds a new seam (a new module other files import, or a new hook registration; CH-34) + the slice's counter-roles k; each re-brief r is a fresh spawn and repeats implementer and `review-verify`; a counter-role that blocked re-runs once per re-brief it caused (P(block) about 0.75). r is 1 to 2 per slice: truemesh S9 needed rounds 3, 3, 2, 2 for its first wave (field report item 4), about 6.8 runs per slice with fresh-spawn re-briefs (r3-pessimistic-realist reference class). Using up two re-briefs is not yet a return: a return needs the result still refuted after the second re-brief (UC-05 step 6.2). P(at least one of the ten stage-2 slices uses up two re-briefs) stays about 0.6 (r1-PR-3); P(a slice still refuted after its second re-brief) is about 0.14 (Laplace from truemesh S9, 0 of 5 slices; r4-PR R4-4), which the return tail uses.

Whole-plan model (author's rebuild for v0.4.4 with the r4- and r5-pessimistic-realist terms, input for the owner's whole-plan threshold; `pessimistic-realist` re-derives it from stage-1 data before stage 2, r3-PR N5, r4-PR R4-4, r5-PR R5-4):

| Part | Median runs | 90th percentile | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| stage 1 (SL-0, SL-1, SL-2, live P1 run) | about 37 | about 43.5 | §11.3 forecast (v0.4.4, +1.5 for v0.4.6) |
| stage-2 slices SL-2b to SL-11 (column "Runs" below) | about 94 | about 127 | per-slice model; adds to v0.4 (86, 116) the outside reviewers of SL-4 and SL-6 (r4-KR G-4), the auditor on SL-5, SL-7, SL-9 (r4-DA N3) and the work moved from SL-1 to SL-8 (§11.3); adds for v0.4.4 the outside reviewer of SL-7 (r5-KR G-I), the C13 clearing branch in SL-5, the `allow` in SL-8 and the SL-4 and SL-6 additions of round 5 (about +2) |
| live-contact term for SL-4, SL-8, SL-11 (new host mechanisms: sandbox, owner commands, team hooks) | about 12 | about 20 | first live contact of a new hook mechanism needed a fix release 4 of 4 times (DECISIONS.md D-14, D-16, D-19, D-20), at least 2 of 4 a second one; P(first fix loop) about 0.8, P(second given first) about 0.4, about 4 runs per loop, so 0, 4 or 8 runs per slice at 0.2, 0.48, 0.32; sum over three slices |
| return tail (CH-37) | about 13 | about 39 | ten stage-2 slices at P 0.14: P(at least one return) about 0.78, P(at least two) about 0.42, P(at least three) about 0.16; about 13 runs per return cycle (P2 `explorer` and `review-verify` about 2, P4 author and counter-roles about 3, re-implementation of a security slice with its reviews and auditor about 7.5; r5-PR R5-4 corrects the r4 unit of 8) |
| §32.25 `devils-advocate` at slice closings with a non-empty `choices:` line | about 4 | about 7 | about 0.4 per slice, ten slices |
| concept amendments after red probes | about 3 | about 9 | about 3 runs per amendment (author, `konzept-reviewer`, `devils-advocate`); one expected among the 20 probes, three at the 90th percentile; every probe runs in SL-0 (§11.1); amendments for reds in the second probe group (§11.3 stop indicators) are spawned after the stage-1 end stamp and count here, those for reds in the first group count in stage 1 (r5-PR R5-2) |
| re-forecast runs | about 2 | about 3 | one before stage 2, one per stage-2 checkpoint |
| live checks per release (§8.3) | about 2 | about 4 | `review-verify` on the live-check record, again after a red row |
| whole plan without SL-12, SL-13 | about 170–174 (v0.4.4: 168–172; +1.5 for the v0.4.6 stage-1 scope) | about 213–223 | before the v0.4.6 addition: sum of the medians about 166, sum of the means about 175; sd about 30 (slice term about 25, return tail about 14, the rest about 7 together); 80 % interval about 137–214; r5-pessimistic-realist put the plan at median about 165–170 and 90th percentile about 210–220 before the v0.4.4 additions of about 2 runs; the sum of the 90th percentiles (about 251) overstates the 90th percentile of the sum |

Discrete branches, not in the sums above:

| Branch | Trigger | Effect on the plan |
|---|---|---|
| A-15 red | `onFailure: "block"` does not block | CDP6 stops at SL-0 (§11.1); the owner decides |
| A-12 red in every mode | the sandbox does not refuse egress | implementation stays refused (PG-11); stage 2 stops before SL-4 for the owner (Q-3 assumed a working sandbox) |
| rule A fails (A-11, A-16, or both A-14 and A-17 red) | team probes | SL-11 not built: about 11 slice runs and about 4 live-contact runs less, whole-plan median about 153–157 |
| the confirmation pass finds a round-5 finding not closed | §0 item 19 (1) | the owner decides; a further author run and the affected roles' re-check cost about 2–5 runs |
| the stage-2 plan blocked at approval | `devils-advocate` (CH-29, CH-34) or `pessimistic-realist` | about 3 runs per further plan round |

Outside the model, shown next to the whole-plan threshold: the concept review so far, about 28 runs (rounds 1 to 5: 20 counter-role briefs and 8 author briefs in `d21/`; r5-PR R5-6), the confirmation pass of §0 item 19 (1) (4 counter-role runs), the v0.4.5 and v0.4.6 author runs (10 `brief-concept*` files in `d21/` with this one) and the threat-modeler closure check on v0.4.5 (1 counter-role run, `brief-cc2-threat-modeler.md`); SL-12 16 runs; SL-13 24–48 eval runs (§0 item 9); the CDP5 fixes after CDP6 (§16, seven findings, not estimated); the owner's probe sessions (not worker runs).

Counting rule for lines: a copied line is a line of a CDP6 file that is identical to a line of its named CDP5 source at 74d17c8, measured per copied file with `git diff --no-index --numstat <cdp5 file> <cdp6 file>` (copied = source lines minus deleted; new or changed = inserted); a file without a CDP5 source counts all its lines as new. Copied and new-or-changed lines are reported separately per slice in the ledger. Copied lines: about 4433 (round-1 measurement: SL-2 1296, SL-4 1677, the rest in SL-3, SL-5, SL-7 and SL-9: `guard-spawn.mjs`, agent files, doctor). Inserted lines include churn: CDP5's history shows 1.30 inserted lines per final line (pessimistic-realist round 1).

Counter-roles (k) follow the owner's staging rule (§11.3): `review-verify` on every slice (not counted in k), `review-code` only for a new seam, `adversarial-auditor` only on a security-boundary slice (one that builds or changes a guard hook, the contract or handback capture, a grant, the sandbox check, an owner-command path, or the enforcement of an owner record: SL-1, SL-2, SL-2b, SL-3, SL-4, SL-5, SL-6, SL-7, SL-8, SL-9, SL-11). SL-6 joins because `integrate.mjs` enforces the veto (OG-6) and `ban-check.mjs` the bans (OG-2, OG-3) (r3-DA O-13); SL-5 because `decision-check.mjs` enforces option bans and vetoes and `trace-check.mjs` scope bans (OG-4, OG-5); SL-7 because it builds the owner commands `relax` and `accept-rejected` and `relax-check.mjs` counts their owner records (AC-06.5); SL-9 because it builds the manifest check at `run.mjs exit` that fails closed on a changed own checker (AC-15.7) and the doctor's sandbox detection (r4-DA N3). The definition is kept, not narrowed: each of these paths is new code in its slice, not a reuse of an audited one. Only SL-0, SL-10, SL-12 and SL-13 run without the auditor (r5-KR G-F). Every slice that builds a triad role text or trigger, the contract or the doctrine also names the owner or a non-conflicted `konzept-reviewer` as outside reviewer (§2).

Copied lines stay about 4433. The run column is the model per slice (median) for orientation; it is not a commitment (§11.3). SL-1 keeps the v0.4 estimate of about +1 run over the r3 figure: v0.4.2 moves `probe --accept` with the accept part of `probe-check.mjs`, `abandon` and the catch-all registration to SL-8 and the file-hash binding to SL-4 (consumer test, §11.3), and adds the `--session-id` transport, the `allow` of UC-11 step 1.2 and the path-argument checks; the author takes the two as equal, which `pessimistic-realist` checks with the stage-1 figure. v0.4.4 moves the `allow` to SL-8 and the C13 clearing branch from SL-2 to SL-5 (consumer test, §11.3) and adds to SL-1 the shared parser with its table and token deny, the hook rules in every session, the `--data-dir` transport and the `--session-id` token deny (r5-TM T5-1, T5-4, r5-KR G-A, r5-DA O5-2): SL-1 net about +0.2, SL-2 about −0.3. The role files added to SL-5 (`threat-modeler`) and SL-6 (`impl-debug`, `impl-refactor`, `review-code`) by the orchestrator's 0.4.3 edit are CDP5 copies of at most 60 lines each with the C13 and `run:` lines changed; they count as copied lines, and their runs stay in the slice figures (r5-DA, r5-PR: about 0 to +1). v0.4.6 (cc2-TM) adds to SL-1 the keyed token with key, nonce record and the `init` settings check, the call-shape rule and the Monitor registration, about +250–350 lines with 18 new fire-test titles (AC-09.15, AC-11.15, AC-11.17), priced at +1 run as the v0.4 addition of comparable size was; to SL-0 the A-22 row, the A-19 prefix and Monitor forms and the key hook, +0.3; SL-4 (AC-13.23) and SL-9 (AC-14.6, AC-14.7) gain checks within their estimates (author's judgment, checked with the stage-1 figure).

### 11.3 Staged commitment (owner decision §0 item 7)

SL-1 alone is not end-to-end: the P1 exit needs a captured devils-advocate verdict stamped before `decided` (UC-01 Exit), and the capture is built in SL-2 (r2-pessimistic-realist). The smallest end-to-end path is therefore stage 1:

1. Every SL-0 row of §11.1: A-1, A-2, A-5, A-6, A-8, A-9, A-11 to A-22 (A-19 plain, compound, prefixed, wrapped and Monitor forms) on the WSL2 host (the team rows may run in the same owner session), A-3 and A-4 on the owner's native Windows instance; none needs built CDP6 code (r4-PR R4-4).
2. SL-1 cut to P1 (with the skill `cdp6-core:idea`) and SL-2 limited to the P1 path (with the role file `cdp6-core:devils-advocate`), as in the table. The ledger names the outside reviewer of SL-1 and of SL-2 (the owner or a non-conflicted `konzept-reviewer`, §2) before each slice starts. The SL-1 outside reviewer also gives the keyed-token fix (UC-11 step 1.3, cc3-TM D-1, D-2, D-5) its independent security check, since the concept's last revision is verified by the orchestrator only (§0 item 20 (3)).
3. One live P1 run in mode `single` at `A0` on a scratch repository, with the contract acknowledged and a captured `cdp6-core:devils-advocate` verdict on `D-1`. `cdp6-core` is enabled only for that session (probe worktree local settings, §1.4 item 8), so a broken guard cannot block the build session or other projects. The sandbox settings of §5.6 are set by hand for it, in the admin-required form through a `--settings` file outside every sandbox-writable path (their check is built in SL-4), and `review-verify` checks them against the settings files. Before the stage-1 start stamp the owner confirms that no local MCP server and no repository hook on the host can read or write files (§0 item 20 (2), §7.4); the confirmation is an owner line `stage-1 host check:` in ledger 0021 with its date and the MCP servers and repository hooks looked at, and the stage-1 start stamp is not set without it. The owner also sets the user-settings rules of §5.6 items 9 and 10 (host key and transcript directory; `Edit` and `Write` deny rules on the run files, the journal, `<data>/` and the installed plugin root), which `run.mjs init` checks (AC-11.17, AC-11.18, AC-09.15); being deny rules, they hold in every permission mode, so no mode condition is added for them (cc2-TM stage-1 gap). Because the SL-4 and SL-8 parts are not built yet, the `review-verify` record of the live run also shows by hand, citing the session transcript (`transcript_path`, hooks.md:734) because `R/tool-log.jsonl` comes from `trajectory.mjs` of SL-4 (r5-TM T5-7): the intake origin is `conversation`, and the transcript holds no `WebFetch` and no Read of a secret path (r3-TM green); every `R/verdicts/` file has a `hooks-fired.jsonl` line of `check-handback.mjs` with the same agent id and capture sha256, since `guard-files.mjs` does not yet deny a main-session Write there (r4-KR G-8); every spawn has its capture, because the reconciliation of §3.4 item 11 needs `trajectory.mjs` (SL-4) and is built in SL-8, so the stage-1 `run.mjs exit idea` records `reconciliation: not built` in its gate result instead of failing or skipping silently (r4-PR R4-2); and every `hooks-fired.jsonl` line shows `permission_mode` `default`, since the catch-all registration that denies `bypassPermissions` for every tool comes with SL-8. A verdict (c) in the live run is answered with `run.mjs object … reject` or left halted for the owner; `abandon` comes with SL-8.
4. Reviews per the owner's staging rule: `review-verify` on every slice; `review-code` only for a new seam; `adversarial-auditor` on SL-1 and SL-2 (both are security-boundary slices, so the rule saves nothing in stage 1).

Consumer test on the stage-1 scope (doctrine §27.1.1, r4-DA N4): an element stays in stage 1 only when a stage-1 step consumes it or a stage-1 safety property needs it. Moved out: `run.mjs probe --accept` with the accept part of `probe-check.mjs` (consumers: `A3`, the owner-command modes beyond `default` and the team readiness check; the live run is `A0` in `default` and needs no accepted record) and `abandon` (consumer: the end of a run; the live run can stay halted) go to SL-8; the catch-all registration (consumers: the red team fault of SL-11, the reconciliation of SL-8, the `bypassPermissions` deny for tools outside the named matchers) goes to SL-8, and stage 1 keeps the named matchers, with the hand check of item 3 for the permission mode; the file-hash binding of owner commands (consumers: `allow-url --file`, `ban --file`, `judge --objections`, `init --import`, `probe --accept`, none in stage 1) goes to SL-4. Kept: `object` (the P1 exit under a (b), (c) or `conflicted` verdict), the probe-record reader (safety property: without an accepted record `default` is the only prompting mode and `A3` is refused, AC-11.5, AC-11.8), the `--session-id` transport and the path-argument checks (safety property: the run lookup and the unsandboxed `run.mjs`, AC-09.11, AC-09.12). Applied again in v0.4.4 to the conflicted round-5 items (r5-DA X-1, r5-KR G-D, r5-PR R5-1): the routine `allow` of UC-11 step 1.2 moves to SL-8, because no stage-1 step needs a `run.mjs` call without a prompt (the live run is attended at `A0`, and CH-49 calls prompting workable there); stage 1 keeps the routine `ask` with the `updatedInput` (hooks.md:1962 pairs `updatedInput` with `ask` or `allow`), and keeps the shared parser, its table and the token deny of UC-11 step 1.2 (safety property: an owner record must not be written by a call the hook read as routine, r5-TM T5-1). The clearing branch of C13 (clearing by a later capture, AC-12.28) moves from SL-2 to SL-5: only `cdp6-core:devils-advocate` exists in stage 1 and `guard-spawn.mjs` denies every other type in a run (AC-12.6), so no stage-1 step can produce a clearing capture; a `conflicted` capture then stays blocking until the owner's `run.mjs object` records (kept), which fails closed. `set-autonomy` stays: the P1 exit of the live run requires the autonomy level and budgets set by the owner (UC-01 Exit and step 4), and AC-11.8 refuses `A3` without an accepted probe record (safety property). Applied in v0.4.6 to the keyed `--hook-token` (cc2-TM F7-1 hardening): it stays in stage 1, in SL-1. Consumers: the stage-1 owner-gate forms `approve`, `set-autonomy`, `object` and `decision … decided --decider owner`, whose owner records the P1 exit of the live run counts (UC-01 Exit, AC-03.10). Safety property: an owner record is written only by a call the hook decided (r5-TM T5-1, kept in stage 1 above); whether PreToolUse fires for Monitor is not in the docs copy (the hooks.md copy does not name Monitor) and the plain v0.4.5 hash is computable by any caller that knows the session id and data dir, so without the key a Monitor call could write an owner record in stage 1. The `init` checks of §5.6 items 9 and 10 stay for the same reason: the key is a secret only under its rules, and the run files are editable by the main session until `guard-files.mjs` (SL-4).

Counting rule for "worker run" (r3-PR N3, r4-PR R4-3): one spawned agent of a build session, counted from two sources between the stage-1 start and end stamps in the ledger: (a) usage-log lines with a non-empty `agent_id` and the `session_id` of a build session named in the ledger (the `cdp5-agents` usage log for the agents that build CDP6, the `cdp6-core` usage log for the live P1 run; `check-handback.mjs:23-32` writes both fields, while the grant lines of `grants.mjs:97` carry neither and do not count); (b) the files `agent-<agentId>.jsonl` under `~/.claude/projects/{project}/{sessionId}/subagents/` of each build session (sub-agents.md:1161). Agent types outside the plugin prefixes, such as `general-purpose`, are not logged (`agent-name.mjs:6-10`) and are counted from (b); for plugin types a difference between (a) and (b) is written into the ledger and (b) counts. Resumes (r5-PR R5-3): a resume by `SendMessage` runs under the same agent id (sub-agents.md:1155), so (b) holds one file for it, and (a) logs one line per agent id, since `check-handback.mjs` returns early once its marker says logged (`:53`) and logs no denied attempt (`:64`, `:69`); stage-1 re-briefs are therefore fresh spawns only, and every `SendMessage` tool call to a subagent in a build session's main transcript counts as one more run and is written into the ledger as a breach of that rule. Implementers, reviewers, counter-roles, outside reviewers and re-runs after a rejected capture count; the main session's own work and the owner's probe sessions do not (owner sessions are reported separately); the concept review rounds before stage 1 are not stage 1. Window (r5-PR R5-2, cc-PR N-2): concept amendments after a red row of the second probe group (A-2, A-3/A-4, A-8, A-12, A-14, A-16 to A-18, the compound A-19 case, A-21) are spawned only after the stage-1 end stamp and count in the whole-plan term of §11.2; amendments after a red row of the first group (A-1, A-5, A-9, A-11, A-13, A-15, A-20, A-22, the A-19 prefixed, wrapped and Monitor forms) and the plain A-19 correction come before SL-1 and count in stage 1. A row is in the first group when its red case forces a concept change before SL-1. Reasons for the rows cc-PR N-2 found ungrouped: A-5 first, because its red case leaves stage 1 without owner commands (`approve`, `set-autonomy`, `object` are SL-1 owner commands and the P1 exit needs them), so another confirmation path must be designed before SL-1; A-9 first, because SL-1 builds the `session_id` binding of §1.4 item 2 (a) and the bootstrap after `compact` (AC-09.3), while the fallback, re-binding by `run.mjs resume`, is built in SL-8, so a red row moves the re-binding into SL-1 or changes the lookup before SL-1; A-2 second, because its fallbacks (agents denied a tool without a path field, `tdd-order.mjs` and C6a as judgment) are already the default of UC-13 step 5 and touch `guard-files.mjs`, `trajectory.mjs` (SL-4) and `tdd-order.mjs` (SL-6), none in stage 1; A-3/A-4 second, because stage 1 runs on the WSL2 host and their fallbacks (CDP6 hooks unsupported on native Windows, `processes: unknown` in `activity-check.mjs`, SL-6) change no stage-1 element; A-22 and the A-19 prefixed, wrapped and Monitor forms first (v0.4.6), because their red cases change SL-1 (the key rule form, a `Read|Grep` key deny in `guard-phase.mjs`) or leave one layer alone against an owner record, which the owner decides before SL-1. A-6 and the plain A-19 case keep their own rows below.

Budget: the owner's first figure was about 20–25 worker runs (§0 item 7); the owner then set the threshold in probability form, P(overrun) at most 0.1 (§0 item 16), set the cap to 38 worker runs (§0 item 18), after round 5 kept that cap while accepting P(overrun) about 0.25 instead of at most 0.1 (§0 item 19 (3)), and on Q-15 raised the cap to 42 worker runs (§0 item 20 (1)): the hard stop at 42 and the checkpoints return control to the owner, and measured stage-1 figures replace the model. Stage-1 forecast for the v0.4.4 scope (author's computation; terms of r4- and r5-pessimistic-realist): the v0.4.3 mean of about 32 (r3 base 26.4 of SL-0 3, SL-1 9 without its outside reviewer, SL-2 9 plus 1 outside reviewer, live P1 run 2 plus a fix loop of 4 at P 0.6; +1 for the SL-1 scope added in v0.4; +2.1 for live fix loops at P 0.8 and a second at P 0.4; +0.9 for a re-plan after a red row among A-1, A-11, A-13, A-15, A-20; +0.5 for the checkpoint re-forecast; +0.8 for §32.25 `devils-advocate` passes at the SL-1 and SL-2 closings); the r5 terms: +1.5 for the grown SL-0 kit and record (the A-5 `ask`, `allow` and `updatedInput` cases, the A-18 deny hook, A-8, A-17, 19 rows), +1.2 for that re-plan priced as a concept amendment of about 3 runs at P about 0.7 (A-11 already contradicted by D-19, DECISIONS.md:249) instead of 1.5 at 0.6, +1 for the SL-1 outside reviewer (§2; the r3 base had none, so SL-1's 9 excludes it, r5-KR question 2), +0.4 for the re-forecast at three or more red rows; the v0.4.4 scope: +0.2 net for SL-1 and −0.3 for SL-2 (§11.2 note). Mean about 36, median about 35.5, 80 % interval about 30–42, 90th percentile about 42, P(more than 38) about 0.3 (right-skewed, sd about 4.6 above the median; r5-pessimistic-realist: mean about 35.5, median about 35, 90th percentile about 41–42, P(more than 38) about 0.25 before the v0.4.4 changes, about 0.35 if second-group amendments counted inside the window, which the counting rule now excludes). v0.4.5: A-5 and A-9 join the first group, at most about +0.2 (cc-pessimistic-realist N-2), and the argv hash of UC-11 step 1.2 is taken as inside SL-1's estimate (author's judgment, checked with the stage-1 figure); the figures above hold within their rounding, P(more than 38) stays about 0.3 against the accepted about 0.25 (§0 item 19 (3), Q-15). v0.4.6 (cc2-TM): +1 for SL-1 (keyed token, `init` settings checks, call-shape rule, Monitor registration; §11.2 note), +0.3 for SL-0 (A-22, the A-19 prefixed, wrapped and Monitor forms) and +0.2 for a re-plan, because the first probe group grows by A-22 and those A-19 forms (P about 0.7 → about 0.75 at about 3 runs): +1.5 in all (author's computation, for `pessimistic-realist` to check), so mean about 37.5, median about 37, 80 % interval about 31–44, 90th percentile about 43.5, P(more than 38) about 0.4 (sd about 4.6 above the median kept), above the about 0.25–0.3 Q-15 asked about. v0.4.7, the basis of the owner's answer to Q-15 (cc3-pessimistic-realist re-derivation of the v0.4.6 scope): on the author's terms, arithmetic-checked, 26.4 → 31.7 → 35.7 → 35.9 → mean about 37.4, median about 37, 90th percentile about 43, P(more than 38) about 0.41; on the outside view (conflicted terms of that role, C13: amendment unit about 4.9 runs at P about 0.75, +1.4; SL-1 security scope +0 to 0.5; checkpoint re-forecast at P about 0.9, +0.4) mean about 39.2, median about 38.75, 90th percentile about 45, P(more than 38) about 0.5. At the cap of 42, P(overrun) is about 0.25 on the outside view and about 0.15 on the author's terms (§0 item 20 (1)). The v0.4.7 additions to SL-1 (token issue time and hash values under the MAC, nonce record at the check, no-print rule, transcript, `<data>/` and plugin-root deny checks, host-key path rule; eight fire tests in AC-09.12, AC-09.15, AC-11.18) and to SL-9 (one warning, AC-14.8) are taken as inside the slice estimates (author's judgment, for the re-forecast to check). Cap: 42 worker runs (§0 item 20 (1); 38 up to v0.4.6), about the 85th percentile on the author's terms and the 75th on the outside view; it stays 42 whatever the figure, and the stop indicators below give the owner the decision when the measured count runs ahead of the model.

Checkpoint after SL-1 (re-based in v0.4.7, cc3-PR condition 2, §0 item 20 (3)): more than 20 cumulative worker runs (SL-0 and SL-1; the v0.4.6 model expects about 18.7–20 there, so 20 is about the 75th percentile and the checkpoint no longer fires on the model alone, as the threshold 15 up to v0.4.6 did at P about 0.9) → re-forecast from the measured SL-0 and SL-1 runs before SL-2 starts; when that re-forecast puts P(stage 1 above the cap) above about 0.25, the figure the owner accepted (§0 items 19 (3), 20 (1)), the owner decides before SL-2 starts whether stage 1 continues, is cut or gets another cap (r4-PR R4-3). The trigger is read on the measured re-forecast only, never on the prior model (at the cap of 38 the model alone gave about 0.41, cc3-PR).

Checkpoint after SL-2 (r5-PR R5-7; re-checked in v0.4.7 against the cap of 42): more than 32 cumulative worker runs, by its rule threshold = cap − (2 + 8), the live P1 run's 2 runs plus its two modelled fix loops of 4 runs each (cc3-PR; 28 = 38 − (2 + 8) at the old cap, 32 = 42 − (2 + 8) now), so a stage 1 at or below the threshold after SL-2 stays within the cap even in the live run's largest modelled case (0, 4 or 8 fix-loop runs); the model expects about 28.5 after SL-2 (v0.4.6) → the owner decides on the measured count before the live P1 run starts; no re-forecast run is spawned for it.

Stop indicators and actions:

| Indicator | Action |
|---|---|
| a red row among A-1, A-5, A-9, A-11, A-13, A-15, A-20, A-22, the A-19 prefixed, wrapped and Monitor forms | re-plan before SL-1 (for A-22 and the A-19 forms with the owner's decision their row names): the fallback of §11.1 for that row goes into the concept as a change, routed to `konzept-reviewer` |
| a red row among A-2, A-3/A-4, A-8, A-12, A-14, A-16 to A-18, the compound A-19 case, A-21 | the fallback of §11.1 goes into the concept before stage-2 plan approval (discrete branches of §11.2); stage 1 continues |
| A-6 red | stage 1 continues with the plugin enabled only through the live run's `--settings`; CH-17 is decided on the result |
| the plain A-19 case red | correct the `excludedCommands` pattern before SL-1; a second red stops stage 1 for the owner |
| three or more red rows of the 20 probes of §11.1 | re-forecast everything before SL-1 |
| more than 20 runs after SL-1 | re-forecast from the measured runs before SL-2 (checkpoint); its P(stage 1 above the cap) above about 0.25 → the owner decides before SL-2 |
| more than 32 runs after SL-2 (cap − (2 + 8)) | the owner decides before the live P1 run (checkpoint, no re-forecast run) |
| a stage-1 slice uses its second re-brief, or the auditor blocks its second pass | the owner decides whether stage 1 continues before the next spawn |
| new or changed lines of SL-1 and SL-2 above 3200 (1.2 × the upper estimates of the table, 1850 + 800; 2750 before v0.4.6, as the v0.3 figure 2300 was for the smaller scope), or inserted per final lines above 1.5 | re-estimate the line figures of stage 2 with the measured ratio |
| the live P1 run without contract acknowledgement or without a captured devils-advocate verdict | stage 1 is not passed; the cause goes to a retro before any stage-2 slice |
| stage-1 worker runs above the cap of 42 (§0 item 20 (1)) | hard stop; the owner decides |

Re-forecast rule (r3-PR N4): the sample is the stage-1 slices plus the five truemesh S9 slices; the re-forecast reports the median and the 90th percentile per slice class (security-boundary, other) and the re-brief count r per slice, rebuilds the whole-plan model of §11.2 with them, and states P(whole plan above the owner's threshold) in probability form. With n=2 security slices in stage 1 the class figures stay wide; the report says so. The owner approves stage 2 on that figure and only with the sandbox admin-required on the host (§5.6 scope rule (a): `allowUnsandboxedCommands` false from managed settings or a counted `--settings` file; §0 item 20 (2)), which `review-verify` checks against the settings files before the approval (the `cdp6-doctor` warning of AC-14.8 comes with SL-9). The staging choice and its review rule go to `devils-advocate` (CH-29, CH-34) before plan approval. The review rule is about building CDP6; OR-14 (two-stage review by size inside CDP6 runs) is unchanged.

| CH-47 | return cycles write under `R/<phase directory>/cycle-<c>/` (r3-DA O-5); a returned P3 or P4 is compared with the hashes in its `returns/` file (§3.2) | a hash baseline per cycle on the same paths (a rewrite is indistinguishable from an edit of a frozen file); a new run per return (owner decision §0 item 13 keeps the return inside the run) | edits of frozen artefacts |

Cost (r5-DA O5-5): a `--setup` command that installs packages needs its registry host in `allowedDomains`, which then opens that host to every sandboxed command of the run, the writers' tests included, for the whole run; each such host needs an owner ruling (§5.6 item 4), and probe A-12 runs `git worktree add` with setup and baseline under §5.6 in SL-0 | a second `excludedCommands` entry for `grant-run.mjs` and `worktree-setup.mjs` (two more unsandboxed paths, and the baseline test run would run unsandboxed); grants outside `<data>/`, for example under `R/` (a second state location for `denyWrite` and `guard-files.mjs`, and the copied `grants.mjs:20-21` path changes anyway) | no grants (auditor counter-tests not executable, UC-06 error case) |

| CH-50 | second layer under the owner gate (cc2-TM F7-1 hardening): `run.mjs` exits 2 without a `--hook-token` whose HMAC-SHA256, under a per-host key that only hooks and the excluded `run.mjs` can read, covers a fresh nonce, the session id, the data directory and the parsed tokens, and refuses a reused nonce; key file `~/.config/cdp6-core/hook.key` written by `bootstrap.mjs`, kept from the model by `denyRead`, `Read(...)`, `Edit(...)`, `Write(...)` and `denyWrite` entries the owner writes into user settings, checked by `run.mjs init` (SL-1) and `cdp6-doctor` (SL-9) (UC-11 step 1.3, §5.6 item 9) | close each hook bypass on its own, as v0.4.4 and v0.4.5 did (T5-1, F6-1 and F7-1 were three bypasses of the one hook layer, so the class stays open for the next tool or form); the plain `--argv-sha256` of v0.4.5 (computable by every caller that knows the session id and data dir); a per-session key file written at SessionStart (a key read through an MCP server then serves one session only, at the cost of one key file per session to create and remove; replaced on Q-16 by the admin-required sandbox before stage 2, §0 item 20 (2)); no nonce (a token stands in the transcript and could be replayed through a path the hook does not see) | the hook decision as the only layer (v0.4.5) |

Answered by the owner on 2026-10-28 (§0 item 20, ledger 0021): Q-15, the stage-1 cap is 42 worker runs (P(overrun) about 0.25 on the outside view, about 0.15 on the author's terms; §11.3, CH-29). Q-16, the residual of CH-50 that processes outside the sandbox can read the host key: accepted for stage 1 on condition that the owner confirms before stage 1 that no local MCP server and no repository hook on the host can read or write files, recorded as `stage-1 host check:` in ledger 0021 before the stage-1 start stamp (§11.3 item 3); before stage 2 the sandbox is admin-required, replacing the per-session-key option, and `cdp6-doctor` warns while it is not (§5.6 scope rule, §7.4, §11.3 re-forecast rule, AC-14.8). ## 16. Findings for CDP5 (for the owner; CDP6 does not copy them)

1. `cdp5-agents/hooks/check-handback.mjs:54-61`: when the contract cannot be verified, the handback is let through and logged as `contract-missing` (fail open). CDP6 denies (AC-12.11).
2. `cdp5-agents/hooks/guard-bash.mjs:452-455`: implementation workers may run `curl` GET; only sending flags are denied. CDP6 denies `curl`, `wget` and `gh api` for writers and requires the sandbox (AC-13.7, PG-11).
3. `cdp5-agents/hooks/hooks.json` has no `onFailure` entry, so every CDP5 guard fails open on a crash, a missing `node` or a timeout (r1-team-facts addendum; hooks.md:904).

4. CDP5 workers can run Bash with `dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true` (sandboxing.md:210) and nothing in `guard-bash.mjs` denies it (r2-konzept-reviewer R1).
5. CDP5 does not guard `SendMessage`: a completed CDP5 worker can be resumed without a new spawn (sub-agents.md:1147), outside the CDP5 counters (r2-konzept-reviewer R2).
6. `cdp5-agents/hooks/guard-spawn.mjs:8` states D-19's diagnosis "the SubagentStart hook does not run for it" as fact in its deny reason; hooks.md:2541 says `SubagentStart` runs per in-process teammate message, and probe A-11 is still open (r3-devils-advocate).
7. `cdp5-agents/hooks/guard-bash.mjs:368` refuses a git subcommand token only for quotes and backslashes (`/[\\"']/`); an unquoted brace expansion such as `{push,x}` is not checked, so the hook and git can read the subcommand differently (cc-threat-modeler follow-up). CDP6 uses a token charset and, since v0.4.6, a keyed hook token for `run.mjs` (UC-11 steps 1.2, 1.3).

Owner decision (§0 item 10): these fixes come after the CDP6 focus.

## Probabilistic statement

CDP6 is a framework whose workers are LLM agents; the hooks and checkers are deterministic Node programs. Probabilistic parts: every role's output (claims, drafts, verdicts) and the main session's orchestration. The deterministic parts bound them: every claim that changes state passes a checker or a captured counter-role verdict, and the gates fail closed.

| UC | Tolerance threshold | Validation strategy | Fallback | EU AI Act risk class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UC-01–UC-08 (phase work by roles) | no unverified claim acts; concept score at or above the policy threshold | deterministic checker per gate plus counter-role in fresh context | halt with typed status; owner | minimal (developer tooling) |
| UC-16 (team conversation) | no message acts outside the header, round and budget rules | message header, team log, captures; a failed team check switches to multi mode (§3.4 item 12) | STOP-6, STOP-7; owner | minimal |
| UC-09–UC-15 (hooks, checkers, scripts) | n/a (deterministic) | unit tests with firing cases, CI on two OS, live check | `fail closed` | minimal |

LLM fields per role class:

| Role class | Context | Output | Validation | Fallback | Cut-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| writers (`author`, `impl-*`) | brief file, cited paths, worktree | diff or draft plus report | checkers, then reviewers | re-brief, then one slice return to P2, then STOP-7 | two re-briefs per cycle |
| reviewers and counter-roles | brief, `target` files at hash | verdict with mapped severity | `verdict-check.mjs` | re-run the role | two rounds (P3, P4 gates: five, then one concept or plan return), then STOP-7 |
| `explorer` | questions, `SRC-n` files, repository | findings with source locators | `sources-check.mjs`, `review-verify` | question marked `open` | four workers per wave |
| teammates | brief, contract per message, messages (data, C3t) | messages with header, own files named in `writes:` | `guard-message.mjs` or `check-teammate.mjs`, then the phase checkers | rebrief, then STOP-7 | message budget per wave |

Knowledge cut-off: a claim about a library, API or host behaviour after the model's cut-off is a question for exploration with a fetched source, never an answer from memory (doctrine §15.5).

## Vendor Risk Statement

| UC | Provider | Model | Migration strategy | Data sovereignty |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| all role work | Anthropic, through Claude Code | per role file front matter (`opus` for counter-roles and reviewers, `sonnet` for writers until O12 decides) | hooks, checkers and run files are plain Node and Markdown, independent of the model; role texts are Markdown; another host would need its own hook adapter (not in scope) | repository content and briefs go to the provider under the owner's Claude Code terms; private stores are denied by `guard-files.mjs`; PII in reports is denied once by `check-handback.mjs` |

## Entity overview

| Entity | File | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Run | `R/run.json` | state machine of the run, phases and cycles, slices, gates, bans `B-n`, vetoes `V-n` |
| Return | `R/exploration/returns/S-n.md`, `returns/plan.md`, `returns/concept.md` | failed attempts or unresolved objections with typed findings of a returned slice, plan or concept |
| Journal | `R/run.journal.jsonl` | replayable state changes |
| Ledger | `R/ledger.md` | human-readable progress, rulings, halts, `choices:` |
| Counters | `R/counters.json` | spawns, rounds, captures, failure streaks |
| Captured verdict | `R/verdicts/*.md`, `index.jsonl` | role final answers with hashes |
| Tool log | `R/tool-log.jsonl` | tool calls of the run |
| Team log | `R/team-log.jsonl` | team messages with header and hash |
| Probe record | `docs/cdp6/probes/<claude-version>.md` (human-readable, gates SL-1); `<data>/probes/<claude-version>.json` (accepted by the owner, gates team mode, `A3` and the owner-command modes) | probe results per Claude Code version |
| Team handover | `R/<phase directory>/team-handover.md` | team work before a switch to multi mode, `unverified` until `review-verify` |
| Decision | `R/decisions.md` | `D-n` register |
| Phase artefacts | `R/idea/`, `R/exploration/`, `R/concept/`, `R/plan/`, `R/briefs/`, `R/reports/`, `R/diffs/`, `R/verification/`, `R/retro/`, `R/loop-back/` | §4 |
| Active runs | `<data>/active-runs.json` | `session_id` and project root → run directory |
| Grants | `<data>/grants.json` | run grants written by `run.mjs grant` |
| Hooks fired | `<data>/hooks-fired.jsonl` | `hooks observed` evidence |
| Usage log | `<data>/usage-log.jsonl` | one line per agent run and manual activity |
| Checker registry | `<P>/checks/registry.json` | phase → checkers, path or referenced plugin, pinned version and script sha256; role → tier tool set |
| Enforcement table | `<P>/enforcement.json` | §7 |
| Manifest | `<P>/manifest.json` | file hashes per release |
| Live-check record | `docs/cdp6/live-checks/<version>.md` | release evidence |

## Glossary

| Term | Definition | Semantic anchor |
|---|---|---|
| `run directory` | the directory `R` holding every file of one run | run record, durable artefacts |
| `run record` | `run.json` with its journal; the state machine on disk | state machine, resume, journal |
| `phase card` | the eight items every phase states (entry, exit, artefacts, executors, gate, anti-drift, standalone, human gate) | phase contract |
| `frozen id` | an id whose artefact hash was recorded at its phase exit; never renumbered | traceability, supersede |
| `gate` | the end of a phase: deterministic part and judgment part | quality gate |
| `captured verdict` | a role's final answer stored by the handback hook with hash and target | evidence binding |
| `counter-role` | devils-advocate, pessimistic-realist, adversarial-auditor; also the reviewers in their gate role | counter-vote, fresh context |
| `brief header` | the machine-readable lines opening every brief and spawn prompt | pointer brief |
| `input-class block` | the failure-class × outcome table in a brief (O6) | fail-closed design |
| `research firewall` | project context shapes questions and never counts as evidence | source provenance |
| `fail closed` | a missing or failing checker or hook stops the action | default deny |
| `single writer` | each run file has exactly one writing program | ownership |
| `autonomy level` | `A0`–`A3`, set per assignment by the owner | human-in-the-loop |
| `stop list` | STOP-1 to STOP-11, always to the owner | human gate |
| `owner record` | a stamped record with decider `owner` that an owner command writes into `run.json`; checkers count owner decisions only from these | owner gate, audit |
| `decision class` | mechanical, taste, user challenge | delegation of decisions |
| `ruling` | a recorded decision with why and cost if wrong | decision log |
| `terminal status` | the typed end of an unattended run | headless halt |
| `trace map` | AC → test names → results from a hashed log | requirements traceability |
| `loop-back` | P8: routes retro output into the next run | Kaizen follow-through |
| `hooks observed` | a line per hook firing, read by doctor and release check | live evidence |
| `synced copy` | an account-synced plugin copy that can shadow the installed one | version drift |
| `intake` | the one input type of the idea phase: kind (idea, feature request, issue, bug report), origin, text verbatim | ticket intake, triage |
| `team lead` | the main session in team mode | agent team lead |
| `teammate` | a `cdp6-core:` role spawned with `name` into the team, in-process | agent team member |
| `message header` | the first line of every team message: run, task, kind, round | message protocol |
| `team gate` | the recorded answer why a team, its roster and its message budget, before the first named spawn | fan-out gate |
| `ban` | an owner record `B-n` that forbids a path, dependency, technology, capability, scope item or decision option; enforced by checkers until lifted | owner constraint |
| `veto` | an owner record `V-n` that stops one phase exit, decision or integration and halts the run | owner gate |
| `slice return` | a slice whose two re-briefs are used up goes back once to P2 with typed findings (`intent`, `plan`, `assumption`) and a new cycle | escalation, re-entry |
| `cycle` | one pass of P2 to P5 inside a run; a slice, plan or concept return opens the next cycle and supersedes ids | iteration, supersede |
| `team fault` | a failed team check at runtime; the phase continues in multi mode | degraded mode |

## Responsibility Matrix

| UC | AI share | Human validator | Validation mindset level | Liability in case of damage |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UC-01 | 40 % | owner (answers, approach choice) | high | owner |
| UC-02 | 80 % | owner at `A0`; else `review-verify` plus owner at the next gate | high | owner |
| UC-03 | 80 % | owner at `A0`/`A1`; konzept-reviewer and devils-advocate otherwise | high | owner |
| UC-04 | 80 % | owner (plan approval at `A0`–`A2`) | high | owner |
| UC-05 | 90 % | owner (integration at `A0`–`A2`); reviewers per slice | high | owner |
| UC-06 | 80 % | owner (relaxations at every level) | high | owner |
| UC-07 | 80 % | owner at `A0` | medium | owner |
| UC-08 | 70 % | owner (next run start) | medium | owner |
| UC-09–UC-15 | 0 % at run time (deterministic); 100 % authored by agents | owner reviews each slice before merge | high | owner |
| UC-16 | 90 % | owner at the team gate and at each phase exit of a team phase at `A0` | high | owner |
| UC-17 | 0 % (owner records, deterministic enforcement) | owner (every ban and veto) | high | owner |
